- Plan evidence, checked on 2026-10-14 in `.venv`, not outcome evidence:
  - `argparse` with a required subparser turns `--bogus` into `the following arguments are required`, so the subcommand must be optional (intent § Constraints);
  - `psycopg` 3.3.6 `_conninfo_attempts._resolve_hostnames` calls `socket.getaddrinfo` in Python for a host that is not an IP address and skips it for `127.0.0.1`, while the connection itself is opened by `libpq`, not through Python's `socket`. A Python socket guard therefore blocks `httpx` but not `libpq`, and it must allow `getaddrinfo` for the configured database host (§ Binding constraints "Tests").

- For Revision 12 (2026-10-22, at `c20827d`, the validated head of PR #26): `audit.md` round 6 (REJECTED: F-27 MEDIUM; F-28, F-29, F-12 LOW; experiments X26 to X31; mutants N10, N11, N12, N31, N38, N39 survive); `interfaces/cli.py` (`_CliParser._print_message`, `_IngestParser`, `_exit_after_writing`, `main`); `interfaces/ingest_command.py` (`_StderrReporter`, `_attempt`, `_settled_code`, `_report`, `_print_running`, `_end_after_escape`, `_migrate`, `_invoke`, `run_ingest_command`: every write is a `print` with no flush); `README.md` § Ingesting lines 290-295 and the exit-code table; `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_command.py` (`RaisingStream` raises on a chosen write only; `Rig._through_command` and the `main` tests); `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_layers.py` (the effectful-module check covers `application` only); `pyproject.toml` (`[tool.pytest]` `testpaths = ["tests"]`, one marker, `integration`). Python 3.13 behavior relied on, from its documentation: stdout to a pipe or a file is block-buffered and stderr is line-buffered (since 3.9); at exit the interpreter flushes `sys.stdout` and `sys.stderr`, reports a failed stdout flush as `Exception ignored ...` and exits 120 when either flush fails; the interpreter ignores `SIGPIPE` at start-up, so a write to a pipe whose reader has gone raises `BrokenPipeError` on Linux (on Windows `OSError`, `EINVAL`, as the auditor's X27 and X28 show); the `signal` module's note on `SIGPIPE` recommends redirecting the descriptor to `os.devnull` with `os.dup2` after a broken pipe.
- For Revision 13 (2026-10-27, at `871f440`, the validated head of PR #26): `audit.md` round 7 (REJECTED: F-31 MEDIUM, blocking; F-30, F-32 to F-36 LOW; F-12 carried; experiment U and the console-script reproduction of F-31; the surviving mutants of § Mutants, C9 among them); `interfaces/output_streams.py` (`neutralize`, `_written_and_flushed`, `write_line`, `write_text`, `_flushed_for_exit`, `flush_for_exit`); `interfaces/cli.py` (`_CliParser._print_message` with `file or sys.stderr` and `suppress(AttributeError)`, `_code_after_writing`, `_code_of` with `parser.print_usage(sys.stderr)`, `main`); `interfaces/ingest_command.py` (`_StderrReporter`, `_attempt`, `_settled_code`, `_report`, `_print_running`, `_end_after_escape`, `_migrate`, `_invoke`, `run_ingest_command`: twelve write sites, all through `write_line`); `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_command.py` (`Rig`, `invoke_through`, `RaisingStream`, `FlushFaultStream`, `record_redirections`) and `tests/process/test_cli_process_streams.py` (the bootstrap reads `sys.stdout.encoding`, so it needs a guard for a missing stdout); `C:\Program Files\Python314\Lib\argparse.py` and `_colorize.py` (3.14.9; intent § Findings of the audit, round 7); the 3.13 source is `unavailable` locally. Experiments (Windows, Python 3.14.9, scratch copies of `871f440`, label `experiment`): `print(file=None)` writes on `sys.stdout`; a launcher that closes descriptor 1 or 2 and then starts the real child gives that child `sys.stdout` or `sys.stderr` `None` (three runs each) and bounds it with its own timeout (a hung child is killed and the launcher exits 96); the 871f440 results with a missing stream, in process and in real processes (closed descriptor and emulation agree in every case); a scratch prototype of the rules of "A missing stream" below, which passed the 2623 unit and process tests of `871f440` and gave the planned results. Counts re-run at `871f440` (`rerun`, Windows): `-k "usage or environment"` 66 and `-k main_error_stream_fault` 27 in `tests/unit/ingest`; no test name contains `absent_` or `process_no_`.
- Baseline: `main` at `2e6edcd`, 1307 tests (1132 passed and 175 skipped without a database; all pass with `--env-file .env`), PT-6.

- `cli`: new requirements for `ingest`, its exit codes and its output; the CLI-2 scenario's usage line gains the subcommand; Invariant 3 is answered; a new requirement (Revision 11, D-3 option B): a Ctrl+C that reaches `main` exits 130 and any other exception exits 1, without a traceback, for every command, while every `SystemExit` passes unchanged. CLI-1 to CLI-3 are unchanged, and `tests/unit/test_cli.py` passes unedited. Revision 12 (F-27): every output of `main` and of the command is written and flushed in one attempt, so a closed or failing stdout or stderr gives the planned exit code (1, or 130, or a `SystemExit`'s own) in a real process; in the normal case, where the neutralizing of the failed stream works, never Python's exit 120 or its `Exception ignored` report; when the neutralizing itself fails, the residual limitation of D-10 applies (Python's finalization may still print its report and replace the agreed code by 120; the stored run and PostgreSQL are unaffected and the faulted output is not attempted again); with working streams nothing changes. At archive, `harny-sync` records in `cli` both the normal protection against exit 120 and the D-10 reservation, as a stated residual limitation and not as a guarantee. Revision 13 (F-31, D-11 and D-12 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-27): a stdout or stderr missing at start-up (`None`) is disabled output for every command: nothing meant for it is written anywhere, and it is never a failure, so the exit codes are those of working streams; `cli` records it at archive.
- `persistence`: PS-7 and PS-13 modified (two port operations); a new requirement for the listing of runs and the exclusion; Invariant 8 retired; reservation N5 noted as safe under the exclusion; PS-4 modified (a busy lock raises `MigrationLockError`); PS-13 modified (six errors: `PersistenceError`, its four direct subclasses, and `MigrationLockError`, a subclass of `MigrationError`).
- `raw-evidence`: RE-12 modified (the listing); a new requirement for the listing; Invariant 7 modified (a listing exists; still no indexing, bulk verification or cleanup).
- `source-access`: no requirement changes. The F4 consumer rules are met, and the reservations R-7 and R-11 may be marked as handled by F4.
- `project-toolchain`: PT-6 and PT-10 counts; PT-3 unchanged; PT-5 keeps its rule, with the F-1 `[tool.mypy]` settings noted.
- A new capability `ingestion` (suggested prefix `IG-`) for the use case: page loop, write order, failure, interruption, reconciliation, resumption, catalogue.

## Binding constraints

### Layer placement (source: ADR 0001, `AGENTS.md`, PT-3, intent AC18)
| Layer | New or changed | May import |
|---|---|---|
| `domain` | `ingestion_catalogue.py` | standard library, `domain` (strict `mypy`) |
| `application` | `ingest_ports.py`, `ingest_use_case.py`; the two ports extended | standard library, `domain`, `application` |
| `infrastructure` | listing in `LocalDiskRawEvidenceStore`; listing and exclusion in `PostgresIngestionRepository`; `system_clock.py` | anything approved; only here `httpx` and `psycopg` |
| `interfaces` | `cli.py`, `ingest_command.py` | `application`, `domain`, `infrastructure`, the standard library; never `httpx` or `psycopg` |

No module under `src/ec_procurement_quality/application/` contains SQL (the upper-case keywords `SELECT `, `INSERT `, `UPDATE `, `DELETE `), a piece of the store layout (`objects/`, `observations/`, `.json`), or the words `advisory` or `pg_` (intent AC18). None does today (checked with `grep` on 2026-10-14; `list_observations` has no slash and is not a layout piece). `application` reaches the store only through `RawEvidenceStore` and the database only through `IngestionRepository` (intent AC12). No new file is added directly under `src/ec_procurement_quality/` (PT Invariant 3).

### Public names and signatures
These are fixed because the test-writer, the executor and the human (who writes the use case) write separate parts that import each other (F1 to F3 precedent). Every annotation is required. Below, `Mapping`, `Sequence` and `Callable` come from `collections.abc`, `datetime` is `datetime.datetime` and `Path` is `pathlib.Path`.

**`ec_procurement_quality.domain.ingestion_catalogue`** (strict `mypy`):
```python
class AuditEventType(enum.StrEnum):
    RUN_STARTED = "run-started"
    PAGE_COMPLETED = "page-completed"
    PAGE_FAILED = "page-failed"
    STORAGE_FAILED = "storage-failed"
    LAST_PAGE_REACHED = "last-page-reached"
    RUN_INTERRUPTED = "run-interrupted"
    UNEXPECTED_ERROR = "unexpected-error"
    OBSERVATION_RECONCILED = "observation-reconciled"
    RUN_CLOSED_BY_RECONCILIATION = "run-closed-by-reconciliation"
    RECONCILIATION_FAILED = "reconciliation-failed"


class PageFailure(enum.StrEnum):
    STATUS = "status"
    INCOMPLETE = "incomplete"
    TRANSPORT = "transport"


class EvidenceStoreName(enum.StrEnum):
    RAW_EVIDENCE = "raw-evidence"
    POSTGRESQL = "postgresql"


class ReconciliationProblem(enum.StrEnum):
    LOCAL_OBSERVATION_MISSING = "local-observation-missing"
    LOCAL_OBSERVATION_DIFFERENT = "local-observation-different"
    LOCAL_OBSERVATION_UNREADABLE = "local-observation-unreadable"
    OBJECT_MISSING = "object-missing"
    OBJECT_INTEGRITY = "object-integrity"
    SEQUENCE_GAP = "sequence-gap"
    POSTGRESQL_OBSERVATION_CONFLICT = "postgresql-observation-conflict"
    LOCAL_LISTING_FAILED = "local-listing-failed"


SCOPE_VERSION = 1
SCOPE_SOURCE = "sercop-search-ocds"
WINDOWS_RESERVED_NAMES: frozenset[str]


@dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class IngestScope:
    base_url: str
    buyer: str
    year: int
    first_page: int
    last_page: int

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, JsonValue]: ...

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, scope: Mapping[str, JsonValue]) -> "IngestScope": ...


def validate_ingest_execution_id(execution_id: str) -> str: ...
```
- `WINDOWS_RESERVED_NAMES` is `con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com0` to `com9` and `lpt0` to `lpt9`, lowercase.
- `IngestScope` construction raises `ValueError` (naming the field, never its value) when:
  - `base_url` or `buyer` is not a non-empty `str`;
  - `year`, `first_page` or `last_page` is not an `int` (a `bool` is rejected) of at least 1;
  - `first_page > last_page`.
- `to_json()` returns exactly `{"scope_version": 1, "source": "sercop-search-ocds", "base_url", "buyer", "year", "first_page", "last_page"}` (intent AC13, Q6).
- `from_json` accepts only a mapping with exactly those seven keys, `scope_version` the `int` 1 and `source` that string, and raises `ValueError` otherwise. A resumption uses it to read its original's scope (AC24 "malformed").
- `validate_ingest_execution_id` returns the id when it matches `ingest-[0-9]{8}t[0-9]{6}z-[0-9a-f]{8}` (32 characters), passes F1's `validate_execution_id`, and is not in `WINDOWS_RESERVED_NAMES`; otherwise `ValueError`.
- `pages_left_to_request(scope: IngestScope, completed: frozenset[int], last_reached: int | None) -> tuple[int, ...]` returns, in increasing order, the pages of `[scope.first_page, scope.last_page]` that are not in `completed` and are not above `last_reached`. `last_reached` is the `page` of the chain's one valid `last-page-reached` event (§ State machine H, step 3), or `None` when the chain has none. An event that fails that validation is never passed here: it makes the chain malformed. § State machine H uses it to compute the resumption's pages and to validate every `first_page` (decision P-4).

**`ec_procurement_quality.domain.persistence_errors`** gains (decision P-1):
```python
class MigrationLockError(MigrationError):
    """Another migration run holds the migration lock."""
```
- It is the only subclass of `MigrationError`; the four direct subclasses of `PersistenceError` are unchanged. There are six errors in all.
- `apply_migrations` raises it exclusively at the branch where `pg_try_advisory_lock(MIGRATION_LOCK_KEY)` returned false, with exactly the existing message `another migration run holds the lock` (fixed text, nothing from the driver; PS-12 holds).
- Every other refusal stays a plain `MigrationError`:
  - the directory, file and history checks (PS-3);
  - a failed file (PS-2);
  - the lock that cannot be taken because the server refused the statement;
  - the bookkeeping table that cannot be created or read.
- `PersistenceConnectionError` cases are unchanged.

**`ec_procurement_quality.application.ingest_ports`**:
```python
class Clock(Protocol):
    def now(self) -> datetime: ...


class ExecutionIdSource(Protocol):
    def new_execution_id(self, started_at: datetime) -> str: ...


class ClosableIngestionRepository(IngestionRepository, Protocol):
    def close(self) -> None: ...


class RepositoryOpener(Protocol):
    def __call__(self) -> ClosableIngestionRepository: ...


class IngestReporter(Protocol):
    def run_started(self, execution_id: str) -> None: ...

    def progress(self, message: str) -> None: ...
```
- `Clock.now()` returns a `datetime` with a UTC offset.
- `RepositoryOpener()` raises `PersistenceConnectionError` when the database cannot be reached.
- The use case passes `IngestReporter.progress` only text it built from fixed words, integers, content hashes, execution ids and `str()` of a project error (F1, F2 and F3 messages are fixed text). For any other exception it passes only `type(error).__name__`.
- **Required progress** (intent AC6 c, AC15, Q9; Revision 6, audit finding F-07). The wording is free; the content of these lines is required, and `run_ingest` calls `progress` at least for:
  1. each page that State machine P processes: one line naming the page and, when a response was stored, its sequence, status and content hash; a page with no response is named with fixed words for that (for example `page 2 no response`);
  2. each database operation, read or write, refused by a `PersistenceError` that is not a connection loss, **in any phase of `run_ingest`** (Revision 7, audit findings F-13 and F-14): the openings of a repository, the ingest exclusion, § State machine T, R, H and I, `start_run`, P step 4, § State machine P "Refused writes", the repeats, re-checks, second opening and second exclusion of § State machine C, the `read_run` after a refused P-2 repeat (C step 5), and every operation of § State machine K;
  3. each `PersistenceConnectionError` (a lost connection), **in the same phases**, and a `PersistenceConnectionError` from `open_repository()` (the first opening, and the second one of § State machine C). Revision 6 required these lines only "from `start_run` on"; intent AC15 requires them for every lost operation, and Revision 7 follows AC15 (intent § Risks, P-2: stderr still reports the lost connection).

  Rules shared by items 2 and 3 (required):
  - *Database operation* means a call of `open_repository()` or of an `IngestionRepository` operation (`try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`, `read_run`, `list_runs`, `list_observations`, `list_audit_events`, `start_run`, `record_observation`, `append_audit_event`, `finish_run`).
  - Exactly one line per failed call: a call that fails once gives one line, and its repeat under § State machine C, if it fails too, gives its own line.
  - The line holds fixed words, the operation's name (`append_audit_event` with the event type, `finish_run` with the status), and the identity: the execution id of the run the operation concerns (the resumption target, a run of the chain, the run `X` being reconciled, the drawn id, or the new run), plus the sequence when one is concerned. An operation that concerns no run (`open_repository`, `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`, `list_runs`) is named alone. Nothing from the error is added except, at most, its class name (intent AC6 c: "names the operation and the identity only"): never `str()` of the error, a value, a URL or a setting.
  - Not a refusal, so no line: a `PersistenceNotFoundError` that a state machine reads as an answer (T: the target names no run; H steps 1 and 2: malformed or contradictory; I: the id is free), and a `False` from `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`.
  - In § State machine K the same line is written when one of its database operations raises any other `Exception` (the cleanup stops at it). A `KeyboardInterrupt` during the cleanup (a second one, or the first after a `MemoryError` or another unexpected exception; Revision 10, sweep finding N-2) and `SystemExit` propagate without a required line.
  - In § State machine C steps 2 and 3 the same line, named alone, is written when the second `open_repository()` or the second `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` raises anything other than a `PersistenceError`: a `KeyboardInterrupt`, a `MemoryError` or any other `Exception` (Revision 8, audit findings F-18 and F-19). `SystemExit` propagates without a required line.
  - Elsewhere in the window of § State machine C (Revision 9, audit finding F-21) no further line is required and none is written for the exception: closing a repository is not a database operation, and an exception that lands while a line is being written, or between the steps, interrupts no database operation. A line interrupted while it is being written may be missing or incomplete; nothing is written in its place, and no line names an operation that was not attempted.

  Suggested forms: `page 1 sequence 1 status 200 sha256 <hash>`, `record_observation refused: run <id> sequence 1 (PersistenceIntegrityError)`, `connection lost during finish_run: run <id>`, `connection lost during list_runs`, `connection lost during read_run: run <target>`. Optional, free: a line per reconciled run, the chain check, the new id.

**`ec_procurement_quality.application.ingest_use_case`** (🧑 the human writes `run_ingest`; names and types pinned here):
```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class IngestRequest:
    scope: IngestScope


@dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class ResumeRequest:
    execution_id: str


class IngestEnd(enum.Enum):
    SUCCEEDED = enum.auto()
    RUN_FAILED = enum.auto()
    NOT_STARTED = enum.auto()
    INVALID_TARGET = enum.auto()
    INTERRUPTED = enum.auto()


@dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class IngestOutcome:
    end: IngestEnd
    execution_id: str | None
    run_status: RunStatus | None
    message: str


def run_ingest(
    request: IngestRequest | ResumeRequest,
    *,
    source: ProcurementSource,
    store: RawEvidenceStore,
    open_repository: RepositoryOpener,
    clock: Clock,
    execution_ids: ExecutionIdSource,
    reporter: IngestReporter,
) -> IngestOutcome: ...
```
- `run_ingest` opens the repository itself through `open_repository`, at most twice per call (the second time only under § State machine C), and closes every repository it opened before returning or raising.
- `execution_id` and `run_status` describe the run this call created, or are `None` when none was created.
- `RUN_FAILED` covers a created run that is `failed` or left `running`, and also an unexpected exception after which the cleanup found the run already finished (§ State machine K, step 1): `run_status` is then the stored status, whatever it is.
- `NOT_STARTED` covers an operational or integrity failure with no run created.
- `INVALID_TARGET` covers a `--resume` target that names no run or is `succeeded`.
- `INTERRUPTED` covers Ctrl+C, with or without a run; after the cleanup found the run already finished, `run_status` is the stored status (State machine K, step 1).
- `run_ingest` returns an outcome for every ending except these three, which propagate (Q11; the list completed in Revision 10, sweep findings N-2 and N-9):
  - a `KeyboardInterrupt` raised during § State machine K, whether K was entered for a `KeyboardInterrupt` (a second one) or for a `MemoryError` or another unexpected exception (§ State machine K, "What the cleanup tolerates");
  - a `KeyboardInterrupt` raised by the final close while a `KeyboardInterrupt` already propagates: it propagates in its place ("The final close" below, last bullet); while a `SystemExit` propagates, it does not (Revision 11, F-24);
  - `SystemExit`, from wherever it comes, the final close after the outcome was determined included ("`SystemExit`" below; Revision 11, audit finding F-24).

  This includes an ending whose final close raises (Revision 9, F-22; "The final close" below): when no exception propagates, the final close never makes one propagate.
- This includes the first `open_repository()` call (Revision 7, audit finding F-16): a `KeyboardInterrupt` there -> `INTERRUPTED` with no run (`execution_id` and `run_status` `None`); a `PersistenceError` -> `NOT_STARTED` (the required progress line names `open_repository`); any other `Exception` (`MemoryError`, a synthetic defect) -> `NOT_STARTED` with a fixed message naming the opening of the repository followed at most by the exception's class name. Nothing is written in any of these cases. From the moment `open_repository()` returns a repository, `run_ingest` closes it on every ending, including a `KeyboardInterrupt` that lands before the exclusion is taken.
- **The final close** (Revision 9, audit finding F-22; required). Closing never changes how the run ended. When `run_ingest` is about to return or raise, it closes every repository it obtained that is not closed yet: normally the current one; also a lost repository, or a second repository, whose earlier `close()` in § State machine C was interrupted by a `KeyboardInterrupt` or raised a `MemoryError` (each such repository is attempted once more; a repository counts as closed once its `close()` returned or raised any other `Exception`). Every such repository is attempted, even when the close of another one raised.
  - An `Exception` from a `close()` here, `MemoryError` included, is suppressed and changes nothing: the outcome already determined is returned unchanged (for example `SUCCEEDED` with `run_status` `succeeded`, exit 0).
  - A `KeyboardInterrupt` during the final close keeps the outcome already determined and records the interrupt: `run_ingest` returns that outcome with `end=INTERRUPTED` (exit 130), the same `execution_id` and the same `run_status`, which is the status the process had already confirmed (or `None` when no run was created), and a message that says the ingest was interrupted (wording free; it may keep the earlier message after it). This follows F-05 (a Ctrl+C after the run was finished keeps the stored status) and F-16 (b) (a Ctrl+C while the source is closed exits 130 and keeps the one result line). Nothing is read from or written to the run because of it.
  - When an exception already propagates out of `run_ingest` (a second `KeyboardInterrupt` during § State machine K, or `SystemExit`), the final close is still attempted, an `Exception` from it is suppressed and the propagating exception continues. While a `KeyboardInterrupt` propagates, a `KeyboardInterrupt` from the close propagates in its place (the close's own exception object), and the command treats it as any escaping `KeyboardInterrupt` (invocation step 6: exit 130, `<id> running` when the run was announced); a `SystemExit` from the close propagates in its place. While a `SystemExit` propagates, it continues whatever the close raises (a `KeyboardInterrupt`, an `Exception` or another `SystemExit`), and each repository's close is attempted once (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1; until Revision 10 the sentence let a `KeyboardInterrupt` from the close replace a propagating `SystemExit`).
  - A `SystemExit` from the final close after the outcome was determined propagates, and the outcome is not returned (Revision 11, F-24; "`SystemExit`" below). It is the one exception to "closing never changes how the run ended".
- **`SystemExit`** (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-21; required once the revision is approved). No adapter of the project raises it; apart from `argparse` and `main` themselves (`--version`, a usage error, the command's exit code), it can come only from a defect or from a library that deliberately ends the process. It is **preserved and propagated**: the code may catch it only to finish closing what is still open (the repositories at the final close, the source at step 7), without letting another exception replace it, and then raises that same `SystemExit` object again; it never suppresses it, never turns it into an outcome, an exit code or another exception, and never writes a line because of it. One rule holds everywhere:
  - *It always wins.* When a `SystemExit` and a `KeyboardInterrupt` (or any other exception) arrive together, in any order, the `SystemExit` propagates and the other exception is dropped; of two `SystemExit`, the first one propagates. This holds in the window of § State machine C ("Two or more exceptions"), in § State machine K, at the final close (while an exception propagates, and after the outcome was determined), at invocation step 7 (the close of the source), while the command writes its result (§ Output, "Writing the result"), during invocation step 1, and in `main()`, whose handler lets every `SystemExit` through (§ Public names, `interfaces.cli`).
  - *What still happens.* Every repository not closed yet is still attempted once by the final close, and the source is still closed at step 7; what those closes raise is dropped. Nothing more is read from or written to the run because of it, and the run keeps its stored state.
  - *What the command reports.* No result line and no stderr line is written after it: stdout holds only a result line written before it, which normally means none (stdout is empty, also after `run <id> started`, and also for a confirmed `succeeded` whose final close raised it); stderr holds the lines written before it. `run_ingest_command` lets it escape, `main()`'s handler lets it through, and the process exits with its code; Python prints no traceback for it (a non-integer code is printed by Python itself, outside F4's output).
  - The combinations, with the stored run in brackets:

    | Where the `SystemExit` arrives | `run_ingest` | stdout, stderr, exit |
    |---|---|---|
    | In the window of § State machine C (a window operation, its line, the close at step 1 or 3), with or without a `KeyboardInterrupt` there or at the final close | propagates; nothing read or written; K not entered; every repository attempted (`running`) | stdout empty; stderr the lines written before (the line of a window operation that raised the other exception included); exit its code |
    | During § State machine K, or from the final close while a `KeyboardInterrupt` from K propagates | propagates in place of the `KeyboardInterrupt` (`running`, or `failed` if K's `finish_run` had committed) | as above |
    | Elsewhere in the run (the transport, the store, the reporter, a repository operation), the final close then raising a `KeyboardInterrupt`, an `Exception` or another `SystemExit` | the first `SystemExit` propagates; the close attempted once (`running`) | as above; no `<id> running`, no `interrupted` |
    | From the final close after the outcome was determined (for example the AC1 run) | propagates; no outcome (`succeeded`, or the determined status) | stdout empty, no final message; exit its code |
    | While the command writes its result (the final message, the result line, a handler's line), alone or after a `KeyboardInterrupt` or an `OSError` of an earlier write | (returned before) | no further write; stdout holds at most the one line already attempted, never a second one and never `running` for a confirmed run; exit its code |
    | At step 7: the source's close raises it, or it propagates and the source's close raises a `KeyboardInterrupt` or an `Exception` | (returned or propagated before) | stdout keeps what was written (no `<id> running` from a handler); exit its code |
    | During invocation step 1 (in `main()`: parsing, `default_wiring()`, a usage error and its write; or the command's own step 1), including from the write of an `interrupted` or `unexpected error` line by `main()`'s handler | not called; nothing built (no run) | stdout empty; no further line; exit its code |

**`ec_procurement_quality.application.raw_evidence_store.RawEvidenceStore`** gains (Q14, intent AC22):
```python
def list_observation_sequences(self, execution_id: str) -> tuple[int, ...]: ...
```

**`ec_procurement_quality.application.ingestion_repository.IngestionRepository`** gains (Q4, intent AC23):
```python
def list_runs(self, status: RunStatus) -> tuple[IngestionRun, ...]: ...


def try_acquire_ingest_exclusion(self) -> bool: ...
```
The eight existing operations keep their exact signatures and behavior (PS-7 to PS-13).

**`ec_procurement_quality.infrastructure.postgres_ingestion_repository`** gains `INGEST_EXCLUSION_KEY: int`, a constant in the signed 64-bit range different from `MIGRATION_LOCK_KEY`. Suggested value: the ASCII bytes of `ecpqingx`, `0x6563_7071_696E_6778`. Integration tests use it to hold the exclusion from outside. It never appears in a message.

**`ec_procurement_quality.infrastructure.system_clock`**: `SystemClock()` with `now()` returning `datetime.now().astimezone()`, and `RandomExecutionIdSource()` with `new_execution_id(started_at)`. The id is `ingest-` + the UTC form of `started_at` as `%Y%m%dt%H%M%Sz` + `-` + `secrets.token_hex(4)`.

**`ec_procurement_quality.interfaces.ingest_command`**:
```python
class ClosableSource(ProcurementSource, Protocol):
    def close(self) -> None: ...


@dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class IngestWiring:
    base_url: str
    make_source: Callable[[Callable[[], datetime]], ClosableSource]
    make_store: Callable[[Path], RawEvidenceStore]
    make_repository: Callable[[PostgresSettings], ClosableIngestionRepository]
    migrate: Callable[[PostgresSettings, Path], tuple[str, ...]]
    clock: Clock
    execution_ids: ExecutionIdSource


def default_wiring() -> IngestWiring: ...


def run_ingest_command(
    arguments: argparse.Namespace,
    *,
    environ: Mapping[str, str],
    wiring: IngestWiring,
    stdout: TextIO,
    stderr: TextIO,
) -> int: ...
```
- `default_wiring()` uses `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` and `SercopSource(base_url=..., now=...)` with the F2 defaults, `LocalDiskRawEvidenceStore`, `PostgresIngestionRepository`, `apply_migrations`, `SystemClock()` and `RandomExecutionIdSource()`.
- `run_ingest_command` returns the exit code. It never lets an exception escape while it writes its result (Revision 10, sweep finding N-7; § Output, "Writing the result"), so it returns an `int` on every path once `run_ingest` has returned or raised, except `SystemExit`. Revision 11 (audit finding F-26, decision D-3 option B): its handlers and its step 1 stay as they are. What its step 1 raises before its handlers (a `KeyboardInterrupt`, an exception other than the `ValueError` of a usage rule, or an exception from the write of its usage error) leaves it and is handled by `main()` (§ Public names, `interfaces.cli`; § Order of an invocation, step 1); a direct caller, such as a test, sees it escape. From the moment its handlers start, nothing but a `SystemExit` leaves it. Revision 13 (audit finding F-31; D-11, D-12): `stdout` and `stderr` may be `None` at run time (what `main` passes when Python started without that stream); a `None` one is disabled output ("A missing stream" below). Suggested, revisable: widen both annotations, and those of the `output_streams` helpers and of `_StderrReporter`, to `TextIO | None`, which keeps every caller valid; while an annotation says `TextIO`, a test that passes `None` uses `typing.cast`.
- Tests inject a wiring. The simulated source is a `make_source` that builds `SercopSource` with an `httpx.MockTransport`, `min_interval=0`, `cooldown=0` and a no-op `sleep`. The test store is a `make_store` that ignores the path it is given and returns a store on `tmp_path`.
- `interfaces` itself never imports `httpx`.

**`ec_procurement_quality.interfaces.cli`**:
- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> None` keeps CLI-3.
- `build_parser()` gains an optional subparser `ingest`: `add_subparsers(dest="command")` without `required=True`.
- For `ingest`, `main` calls `run_ingest_command(..., environ=os.environ, wiring=default_wiring(), stdout=sys.stdout, stderr=sys.stderr)` and ends with `raise SystemExit(code)`, so every path ends through `SystemExit` (CLI-3).
- A bare command calls `parser.print_usage(sys.stderr)` and raises `SystemExit(2)`.
- `--version` and `--bogus` are unchanged (CLI-1, CLI-2).
- **The handler of `main`** (Revision 11, audit finding F-26, decision D-3 option B, chosen by the human on 2026-10-21, with D-4 option 1; required once the revision is approved). One `try` around the whole body of `main` (building the parser, `parse_args`, `default_wiring()`, the call of `run_ingest_command`, the bare-command usage and the final `raise SystemExit(...)`), with exactly two handlers:
  - `KeyboardInterrupt` -> one attempt to write `interrupted` on `sys.stderr`, then `raise SystemExit(130)`;
  - any other `Exception` (for example a `MemoryError`, or an `OSError` from the write of a usage error) -> one attempt to write `unexpected error: <class name>` on `sys.stderr`, then `raise SystemExit(1)` (D-4 option 1);
  - what that one attempt raises: a `KeyboardInterrupt` makes the code 130; a `SystemExit` propagates in its place (D-1); any other exception is suppressed. Nothing is written a second time, and nothing is written on stdout.

  It does not catch `SystemExit`: `argparse`'s exits (0 for `--version` and `--help`, 2 for a usage error), the bare command's 2, `run_ingest_command`'s exit code and a D-1 `SystemExit` pass through it unchanged (CLI-1 to CLI-3). It delegates and stays minimal: it never calls `run_ingest_command` again, never writes a result line, never reads or writes the environment, the store or the database, and never builds anything. `run_ingest_command` keeps its own handlers; because nothing but a `SystemExit` leaves them, the handler of `main` acts only on `main`'s own steps, on the command's step 1 (§ Order of an invocation, step 1), and on an interrupt that lands between the command's return and `main`'s `raise SystemExit(code)` (exit 130 then; the result line already written stays the only one).
  - D-5 (option a, chosen by the human on 2026-10-21; intent § Findings of the audit, round 5): Python 3.13's `argparse` suppresses an `OSError` from its own writes (`ArgumentParser._print_message`), so without a change such an error would never reach the handler. **One local parser class** (D-5 option a; required implementation constraint): the adaptation lives in one `argparse.ArgumentParser` subclass of this repository, in `interfaces/cli.py` (the existing `_IngestParser`, or a CLI parser class that `_IngestParser` extends), and that one class is used both for the main parser built by `build_parser()` and for the `ingest` subparser (`add_subparsers(..., parser_class=...)`). It overrides only the writing hook (`_print_message`) so that an `OSError` from the write is not suppressed and reaches the handler of `main`; it writes the same text to the same stream as `argparse` does and copies none of `argparse`'s other logic (formatting, `print_usage`, `error`, `exit`, the `--version` and `--help` actions stay `argparse`'s). `argparse` is not changed globally: no monkeypatching of the `argparse` module, of `argparse.ArgumentParser` or of its `_print_message`, and no other parser of the process is affected. This touches the shipped `cli` capability (CLI-1 to CLI-3 hold with working streams; a write failure of `--version` or `--help` now exits 1 instead of 0, intent D-5). Revision 13 (audit finding F-31; the adjustments (a) to (d) accepted by the human on 2026-10-27): "overrides only the writing hook" now reads "overrides the writing hook and `print_usage`, and nothing else", as the next bullet says.
  - **D-5 as adjusted for a missing stream** (Revision 13, audit finding F-31; the adjustments (a) to (d) accepted by the human on 2026-10-27; required once the revision is approved; intent § Findings of the audit, round 7). `argparse` (3.14.9, read) resolves streams in two places before the hook: `_print_message` does `file or sys.stderr`, and `print_usage` and `print_help` replace a `None` file by `sys.stdout`; `error()` calls `print_usage(sys.stderr)`. So with a missing stderr the usage of a usage error reaches the hook as `sys.stdout`, and with a missing stdout `--version` and `--help` fall back to stderr (experiment). The one local parser class therefore:
    - (a) in its writing hook, writes a message only when its stream exists: a `None` file writes nothing and is not replaced by the other stream; a call without a file means `sys.stderr`, as in `argparse`, and a missing `sys.stderr` then writes nothing too;
    - (b) overrides `print_usage` as well, so that an explicit `None` file (a missing stream) stays missing, while a call without a file still means `sys.stdout`, as in `argparse`; it writes `format_usage()` through the hook and copies nothing else of `argparse`. This covers `error()` and the bare command's `parser.print_usage(sys.stderr)`;
    - (c) keeps suppressing `AttributeError` from the write, as `argparse` does, now only for a stream object that exists but has no `write`; a `None` stream never reaches the write. An `OSError` still reaches the handler of `main` (D-5 option a, unchanged).
    - (d) overrides nothing else: `print_help`, `error`, `exit`, `format_usage`, `format_help` and the `--version` and `--help` actions stay `argparse`'s (`print_help` hands the hook a `None` file when `sys.stdout` is missing, which the hook then drops); `argparse` is not patched globally (no monkeypatching of the module, of `ArgumentParser`, of its `_print_message` or of its `print_usage`); the check that `argparse.ArgumentParser._print_message` is the standard library's own after `build_parser()` covers `argparse.ArgumentParser.print_usage` too;

**Protected writes and the last flush** (Revision 12, audit finding F-27, option (b) chosen by the human on 2026-10-22; decisions D-6 and D-7, option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-22; D-10, option A, chosen by the human and recorded in `docs/learning/day-20-handoff.md`; required once the revision is approved). In a real process stdout to a pipe or a file is block-buffered, so a `print` only fills Python's buffer and a closed pipe shows only when Python flushes at exit (`Exception ignored ... flushing sys.stdout`, exit 120). A failed stderr write leaves its text buffered as well, and the exit-time flush of stderr then fails silently with 120.
- *What is protected.* Every write that the command or `main` makes to stdout or stderr:
  - the result line (`_report`'s outcome line, and a handler's `<id> running`);
  - every stderr line of the command: the final message, the handlers' `interrupted` and `unexpected error: <class>`, the configuration message of step 2, the migration names, the command's own usage error `error: <message>`, and the `run <id> started` and progress lines that `_StderrReporter` writes for the use case;
  - the line of `main`'s handler;
  - every message of the one local parser class (D-5): `--version`, `--help` of the main parser and of `ingest`, the usage and error message of a usage error, and the bare command's usage.
- *The protected write* (one helper in `interfaces`, standard library only; D-7 option A: `interfaces/output_streams.py`; its name and signature are free). It writes the text with the same `write` calls as today (for a line, what `print` writes: the text, then the line end; for a parser message, the one `write` of `_print_message`), then calls the stream's `flush()`, inside the same single attempt and before it returns or raises. So a failure that a buffered stream would show only at exit is raised by that attempt, and the approved rules handle it where they already do: N-7's one attempt and exit code, the command's handlers, the reporter's exception reaching the use case as today, the handler of `main` for the parser's messages (D-4, D-5). On success it returns as today. It never writes a text twice.
- *On failure.* If the write or the flush raises an exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` (an `OSError`, `BrokenPipeError` included; a `ValueError` of a closed file; any other `Exception`), the helper neutralizes that stream and then raises the same exception object. A `KeyboardInterrupt` or a `SystemExit` is raised again unchanged and the stream is not neutralized there (it may still work; the last flush below covers what stays buffered).
- *Neutralizing a stream* (D-6 option A, chosen; the effect is required, the code is the executor's). When the stream has a working file descriptor (its `fileno()` returns one), that descriptor is redirected to the null device: a descriptor opened on `os.devnull` for writing is copied onto it with `os.dup2` and then closed. The stream object, `sys.stdout`, `sys.stderr` and their buffers stay as they are, so, once the redirection has succeeded, whatever Python pushes into that descriptor later is discarded and cannot fail. A stream with no file descriptor (`io.StringIO`, the tests' raising `TextIO`, `capsys`'s streams) is not touched. A failure of the neutralizing itself follows D-10 below. It runs the same way on Windows and Linux. Later writes to a neutralized stream succeed into the null device; it is the command's own logic (`result_written`, one attempt), not the null device, that prevents a second attempt of the result line.
- *When the neutralizing fails* (Revision 12, decision D-10, option A, recommended and chosen by the human, recorded in `docs/learning/day-20-handoff.md`; required once the revision is approved). The neutralizing is attempted at most once per stream in the process, whether it succeeds or not; the helper and the last flush of `main` share that record, and no later failure of the same stream starts a second attempt.
  - It absorbs every `Exception` that the attempt raises: in practice an `OSError` (`os.open` of `os.devnull` or `os.dup2` refused, `io.UnsupportedOperation` from `fileno()`, which is also a `ValueError`), a `ValueError` (a closed stream, a bad descriptor), an `AttributeError` (a stream object with no `fileno`) or a `TypeError`. A stream with no usable descriptor counts as attempted and is left as it is. The absorbed exception replaces nothing: the helper still raises the original write or flush exception, the last flush still drops its own, and the caller's approved rule decides the code (1, or 130 when already determined).
  - It does not absorb a `KeyboardInterrupt`: one raised during the attempt propagates in place of the original exception and is handled by the caller's existing rule (130: `_attempt` and the command's handlers, `main`'s handler, the use case's Ctrl+C rules for a progress line, the last flush of `main`). It does not absorb a `SystemExit` either: one raised during the attempt propagates under D-1 (the first `SystemExit` wins; it is dropped while another `SystemExit` propagates through `main`'s last flush).
  - It writes nothing: no line about the failure, on the broken stream or on the other one. It never retries the faulted write, and the result line is never attempted again.
  - What remains is outside F4's control and is recorded as a residual limitation, not a guarantee. When the redirection failed and the failed stream still holds buffered bytes, Python's own finalization may flush it again, print its `Exception ignored ...` report on stderr (when stderr works) and end the process with exit code 120. `main` still leaves through the same `SystemExit` with the agreed code, raises nothing else, writes no traceback and makes no second attempt; only the interpreter's finalization, which comes after `main`, can replace the code. On this machine a scratch child whose `os.dup2` raised showed exactly that (Windows, label `experiment`, scratch, 2026-10-22).
- *The last flush of `main`* (required). As the very last step of `main`, on every path (its own `SystemExit(code)`, its handlers' exits, and a `SystemExit` that propagates through it: `argparse`'s 0 or 2, a D-1 `SystemExit`), `main` flushes `sys.stdout` and then `sys.stderr`, each in its own guard:
  - an exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` there neutralizes that stream and is dropped;
  - a `KeyboardInterrupt` there neutralizes that stream; it makes the exit code 130 when `main` is ending with its own code, and is dropped when a `SystemExit` that `main` did not raise propagates (D-1);
  - a `SystemExit` raised there propagates in place of `main`'s own code, and is dropped while another `SystemExit` propagates (the first one wins, D-1).

  This step writes no text, retries no output, and changes no code except as just said. Its neutralizing is the same one-attempt neutralizing of D-10: a stream already attempted is not attempted again. It may push out bytes that an interrupted attempt had already handed to a working stream: that is the same attempt, not a second one.
- *A missing stream* (Revision 13, audit finding F-31; D-11 option A and D-12 option A, both chosen by the human on 2026-10-27; required once the revision is approved). A stream is *missing* when it is `None`: `sys.stdout` or `sys.stderr` when Python started without a valid descriptor 1 or 2 (for example `>&-` or `2>&-`; CPython then sets the attribute to `None`), or a `None` handed to `run_ingest_command` as `stdout` or `stderr`. A missing stream is disabled output, never a failing stream:
  - *The protected write.* For a missing stream it writes nothing, neither there nor on the other stream (in particular `print(text, file=None)`, which falls back to `sys.stdout`, is never reached), flushes nothing, raises nothing and returns as a successful write does. There is no attempt to count: the caller's own records still hold (`result_written` is set before the write, so no second result line is ever attempted), and the caller's rules see no failure (`_attempt` returns no error, `_settled_code` keeps the outcome's code, the use case's N-6 rules for a raising reporter never fire, and the command's step 1 returns 2 for a usage error).
  - *The neutralizing and the record of D-10.* A missing stream is never neutralized: no `fileno()`, no `os.open`, no `os.dup2`, and no entry in the once-per-stream record. Called with `None`, the neutralizing does nothing.
  - *The last flush of `main`.* A missing stream is skipped: nothing is flushed, nothing is neutralized, and it contributes no `KeyboardInterrupt` or `SystemExit`, so the code `main` leaves with is the one the other stream and the earlier steps give.
  - *The parser's messages.* As "D-5 as adjusted for a missing stream" above: a message meant for a missing stream writes nothing and never reaches the other stream.
  - *What it changes.* Nothing else: the exit code, the outcome, the run and its events, and every byte written on the stream that exists are those of an invocation whose missing stream works. In particular a usage, configuration or resumption-target error exits 2 with stdout empty, an ingest runs to its outcome (no `unexpected-error`, no `failed` run because of it), and the handler of `main` still gives 130, 1 or the `SystemExit`'s own code with its line not written.
  - *A missing stdout* (D-12 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-27): the result line, `--version`, `--help` and `ingest --help` are not written, and nothing is written on stderr in their place; the exit code is the one the command determined (for example 0 for a `succeeded` run and for `--version`).

  | Producer (code with working streams) | stderr missing | stdout missing (D-12) | both missing |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Result line after `SUCCEEDED` (0), `RUN_FAILED` (1), `INTERRUPTED` (130), or a handler's `<A> running` (130 or 1) | the same code; stdout exactly the one line | the same code; the line not written; stderr as with a working stdout | the same code; nothing written |
  | Progress, `run <id> started`, the final message, migration names, the configuration message | not written; no failure; the run as with a working stderr | written on stderr | not written |
  | `error: <message>` of the command, a usage error of `argparse`, the bare command's usage (2) | 2; stdout empty | 2; usage and error on stderr | 2; nothing written |
  | `--version`, `--help`, `ingest --help` (0) | 0; the text on stdout | 0; nothing written anywhere | 0; nothing written |
  | `main`'s handler (130, 1, a `SystemExit`'s own code) | the same code; its line not written | the same code; its line on stderr | the same code; nothing written |
- *What does not change.* With working streams every byte, its order and its stream are as today: `tests/unit/test_cli.py`, the in-process `main` and command tests, the `cli_` integration tests and CLI-1 to CLI-3 hold (`--version` 0, `--help` 0, usage errors 2, the bare command 2). The one local parser class keeps `argparse`'s behavior for a working stream and changes only its writing hook (D-5); `argparse` is not changed globally. `application`, `run_ingest` and the outcome are not touched, no exception becomes an outcome, and the exit codes are those of § Exit codes. When the neutralizing succeeds (the normal case: the null device opens and is copied onto the descriptor), the process never ends with Python's `Exception ignored ... flushing sys.stdout` report, and Python's exit code 120 is never a result. When it fails, the residual limitation of D-10 applies.
- *Outcome table.* "Fails" means a write or a flush raises an `OSError` (`BrokenPipeError` on Linux, `OSError` with `EINVAL` on Windows, for a pipe whose reader has gone) or a `ValueError` of a closed stream. In every cell: no traceback written by F4; no `Exception ignored` report and no exit 120 when the neutralizing of the failed stream succeeds (the normal case; otherwise D-10's residual limitation); no second attempt of the faulted output, and nothing read from or written to PostgreSQL because of it, so the stored run keeps the status the use case left (`succeeded`, `failed` or `running`). `<cls>` is `OSError` on Windows and `BrokenPipeError` on Linux.

  | Producer (code with working streams) | stdout fails | stderr fails | both fail | Ctrl+C in the write or flush | `SystemExit(n)` in the write or flush |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Result line after `SUCCEEDED` (0; stored `succeeded`) | 1; the reader gets no line (or a partial one); stderr keeps the final message | 1; stdout exactly `<A> succeeded`; the final message missing or partial | 1; nothing more is seen | 130; stdout at most the one attempt | n; no further write |
  | Result line after `RUN_FAILED` (`<A> failed` or `<A> running`; 1) | 1, as above | 1, as above | 1 | 130 | n |
  | Result line after `INTERRUPTED` (130, the final close's Ctrl+C included) | 130 (already determined) | 130 | 130 | 130 | n |
  | A handler's `<A> running` and its stderr line (130 for an escaping Ctrl+C, 1 otherwise) | 130 or 1, as determined | 130 or 1 | 130 or 1 | 130 | n |
  | Other stderr lines of the command (progress, `run <id> started`, migration names, the configuration message, `error: <message>`) | not written to stdout | the exception reaches its caller as a write that raises does today: during the run the use case's rules (an exception from the reporter; N-6), before the run the command's or `main`'s handler, so 1; stderr is neutralized and later stderr lines are discarded | as stderr | 130 | n |
  | `--version` (0) | 1; stderr exactly one `unexpected error: <cls>` | 0 (nothing is written to stderr) | 1; nothing seen | 130; stderr `interrupted` at most once | n |
  | `--help` and `ingest --help` (0) | 1; stderr exactly one `unexpected error: <cls>` | 0 | 1 | 130 | n |
  | Usage error written by `argparse`, and the bare command's usage (2) | 2 (nothing is written to stdout) | 1; the handler's own line is discarded | 1 | 130 | n |
  | `main`'s handler line (`interrupted` 130, `unexpected error: <class>` 1) | not written to stdout | the code already determined (130 or 1) | the same | 130 | n, that same object (D-1) |

### CLI arguments (intent Q9, AC14)
- Fresh run: `ingest --year <int> --buyer <text> --last-page <int> [--first-page <int>] [--migrations-dir <path>]`. `--first-page` defaults to 1, `--migrations-dir` to `migrations`.
- Resumption: `ingest --resume <execution id> [--migrations-dir <path>]`.
- `argparse` types parse integers. Every rule `argparse` cannot express ends with `parser.error(...)`, which is exit 2 with the usage line, on stderr, before anything else happens:
  - a missing `--year`, `--buyer` or `--last-page` on a fresh run;
  - `--resume` together with any of `--year`, `--buyer`, `--first-page`, `--last-page`;
  - an `IngestScope` rule;
  - a `--resume` id outside the F1 pattern.
- There is no `--raw-root` option and no base-URL option (Q8). `argparse` reports an unknown option as `unrecognized arguments` (exit 2).

### Configuration (intent Q8, AC14, AC25)
- `run_ingest_command` reads only `environ`; nothing reads `.env`.
  - Required: `POSTGRES_DB`, `POSTGRES_USER`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`.
  - Optional: `POSTGRES_HOST` (absent -> `127.0.0.1`) and `POSTGRES_PORT` (absent -> `5432`; present but not a decimal integer -> exit 2).
  - `PostgresSettings` validates the rest (a port outside 1 to 65535 -> `ValueError` -> exit 2).
  - Messages name the variable or field and never the value.
- Paths are resolved once, at the start of `run_ingest_command`, before anything else is touched:
  - `store_root = Path.cwd() / "data" / "raw"`;
  - the migrations directory is `Path.cwd() / <--migrations-dir>` when relative, or as given when absolute.
  - Both are absolute from then on; nothing later reads the current directory (intent AC25).
  - There is no detection of the repository root (finding 21).

### Order of an invocation (intent Q9; exit code of each step's failure in brackets)
1. Arguments (2), environment (2), absolute paths. **Where step 1 runs** (Revision 11, audit finding F-26, decision D-3 option B and D-4 option 1, chosen by the human on 2026-10-21; required once the revision is approved):
   - In `main()`, before the command: `build_parser().parse_args(argv)` (`argparse`, whose `ingest` parser also applies the rules of `request_from_arguments` and ends a violation through `parser.error`, that is usage on stderr and `SystemExit(2)`), then `default_wiring()`, which builds no source, store, repository or connection.
   - In `run_ingest_command`, before its handlers (unchanged): `request_from_arguments` again (the namespace may come from a caller other than `main`), the reading of `environ` and the building of `PostgresSettings`, and the write of a usage error (`error: <message>`, exit 2). The resolution of the absolute paths is already inside its handlers.
   - All of it is inside the handler of `main` (§ Public names, `interfaces.cli`), which gives the outcomes:
     - a `KeyboardInterrupt` anywhere in step 1 (during `parse_args`, during `default_wiring()`, during the command's checks, or while a usage error is written by `argparse` or by the command) -> exit 130 through `SystemExit(130)`, stdout empty, stderr `interrupted` attempted once after what was already written;
     - any other exception that is not a usage error (for example a `MemoryError`) -> exit 1 through `SystemExit(1)`, stdout empty, stderr `unexpected error: <class>` attempted once;
     - an exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` raised while a usage error is written -> exit 1 (D-4 option 1), the message possibly partial or missing and never written again; an `OSError` from a message that `argparse` writes gives 1 too (D-5 option a, through the one local parser class of § Public names, `interfaces.cli`);
     - a usage error with working streams -> exit 2 as before (`argparse`'s `SystemExit(2)`, or the command's return 2 turned into `SystemExit(2)` by `main`);
     - `SystemExit` is preserved and propagated ("`SystemExit`" in § Public names).

     In every one of these cases nothing is built (no `make_source`, `migrate`, `make_store` or `make_repository` call), nothing is requested, read from the database or written, the ingest is not run, and no traceback is printed.
2. Prepare the source, before anything is written (Revision 6, audit finding F-03):
   1. Keep the `httpcore` logger above `DEBUG`: if `logging.getLogger("httpcore").getEffectiveLevel() <= logging.DEBUG`, set that logger's own level to `logging.WARNING` and restore it when the invocation ends. No handler is added and nothing else is configured (R-7, intent AC15).
   2. `source = wiring.make_source(clock.now)`. From here on the source is closed in a `finally` (step 7), also when step 3 stops the command. The outcomes:
      - any `Exception` other than `MemoryError` -> 2, with the fixed configuration message below; no migration is applied, no store or repository is built, nothing is requested and nothing is written (intent AC14, binding constraint "report a malformed proxy or certificate setting in the environment as configuration");
      - `MemoryError` -> 1 (`unexpected error: MemoryError`); `KeyboardInterrupt` -> 130; `SystemExit` is preserved and propagated (Revision 11, F-24; until Revision 10: "is not caught").

      Why the catch is broad: `interfaces` may not import `httpx` (§ Layer placement), and `SercopSource` lets the library's own construction errors propagate unchanged (F2 plan, row C15). With `default_wiring`, every argument of `SercopSource` is a code constant, so a construction failure can only come from the environment's proxy or certificate settings: `httpx.InvalidURL` (not a `ValueError`) or `ValueError` for a malformed `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY` or `ALL_PROXY`, `ImportError` for a SOCKS proxy, and `FileNotFoundError` or `ssl.SSLError` (both `OSError`) for `SSL_CERT_FILE` or `SSL_CERT_DIR` (§ Guidance consulted, probe of 2026-10-18).

      The message (required content; wording free): fixed text naming the setting kind (proxy or certificate setting of the environment) and the six variable names `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `SSL_CERT_FILE` and `SSL_CERT_DIR`, optionally followed by the exception's class name. Never `str()` of the error (`httpx` quotes the URL), a value, a path or the chain (R-6). Suggested: `error: the source cannot be built from the proxy or certificate settings of the environment (HTTP_PROXY, HTTPS_PROXY, ALL_PROXY, NO_PROXY, SSL_CERT_FILE, SSL_CERT_DIR): InvalidURL`.
3. `wiring.migrate(settings, migrations_dir)`, which names the applied files on stderr. The outcomes:
   - `MigrationLockError` (the migration lock held by another session) -> 1;
   - any other `MigrationError` (a directory, file or history mismatch) -> 2. `interfaces` catches `MigrationLockError` before `MigrationError`;
   - `PersistenceConnectionError` or `PersistenceError` -> 1.
4. `make_store(store_root)`, then `run_ingest(...)` with the source of step 2 and `open_repository = lambda: wiring.make_repository(settings)`.
5. Inside `run_ingest`:
   1. Open the repository (`NOT_STARTED`; a `KeyboardInterrupt` -> `INTERRUPTED` with no run; § Public names, `run_ingest`, Revision 7).
   2. `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()`; `False` -> `NOT_STARTED`, nothing written; a refusal or a lost connection -> `NOT_STARTED`, nothing written, with the required progress line naming `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion` (Revision 7).
   3. For `--resume`, the target check of § State machine T (`INVALID_TARGET`).
   4. Reconciliation of every `running` run (§ State machine R; `NOT_STARTED` on a problem).
   5. For `--resume`, the chain checks (§ State machine H; `NOT_STARTED`).
   6. `started_at = clock.now()`, then a new id (§ State machine I; `NOT_STARTED`).
   7. `start_run`, then `reporter.run_started(id)`, then `run-started`.
   8. The page loop (§ State machine P).
   9. `finish_run`.
6. Map the outcome to an exit code and write stdout (§ Output). When an exception escapes `run_ingest` after `reporter.run_started(id)` was called, whatever it is, stdout is `<id> running` (the last status the process confirmed; closing not confirmed, decision P-5): exit 130 for a `KeyboardInterrupt`, and exit 1 with only the class name on stderr for any other exception (Revision 6, audit finding F-04). Before `run_started`, stdout stays empty. `SystemExit` is preserved and propagated, and no line is written after it ("`SystemExit`" in § Public names; Revision 11, F-24). An exception raised while step 6 writes (the final message, the result line, or the lines of the two handlers above) follows § Output, "Writing the result" (Revision 10, sweep finding N-7).
7. Close the source in a `finally`. Any `Exception` from `close()` is suppressed and changes nothing. A `KeyboardInterrupt` during `close()` (Revision 7, audit finding F-16) -> exit 130 and stderr `interrupted`, and stdout keeps exactly what step 6 already wrote: the result line is written at most once per invocation, so when step 6 wrote it, no second line (`<id> running` or any other) is added; when step 6 wrote none because an exception escaped `run_ingest` after `run_started`, the one line is `<id> running`; when no run was announced, stdout stays empty. While a `SystemExit` propagates, the source's close is still attempted, and a `KeyboardInterrupt` or an `Exception` from it is dropped, so the `SystemExit` continues; a `SystemExit` raised by the source's close propagates, also in place of a `KeyboardInterrupt` that was propagating, and stdout keeps what step 6 already wrote (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1; "`SystemExit`" in § Public names).

### Exit codes (intent Q9; finding 22)
| Code | When |
|---|---|
| 0 | `IngestEnd.SUCCEEDED`: the run is `succeeded`, including a run confirmed `succeeded` by the exact repeat of its final `finish_run` after a lost connection (§ State machine C, decision P-2) |
| 1 | `RUN_FAILED`: a run was created and is `failed` or left `running`; this covers every connection loss except the P-2 case. `NOT_STARTED`: no run created, because the database was unreachable, the migration lock was busy (`MigrationLockError`), the exclusion was held, reconciliation failed, the chain was cyclic, malformed or contradictory, or no free id was drawn; also a database read or write refused or lost before the run exists, including the target's `read_run` (never exit 2 for it), and a non-database exception while the repository is opened (Revision 7, F-15, F-16). Any exception escaping `run_ingest` other than `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit`, with stdout `<id> running` when the run was announced (step 6, F-04). A `MemoryError` while the source is built (step 2). An `Exception` that reaches the handler of `main` (in step 1: during `parse_args`, `default_wiring()` or the command's checks, other than the usage error itself), and an exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` that prevents writing a usage error of step 1 (Revision 11, audit finding F-26, decisions D-3 option B and D-4 option 1; for an `OSError` that `argparse` would suppress, D-5 option a). An exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` (`OSError`, `MemoryError`, any `Exception`) that prevents the command from writing its result at step 6, unless 130 was already determined; the run keeps its stored status, so a run may stay `succeeded` in PostgreSQL while the command exits 1 (Revision 10, sweep finding N-7, the human's decision of 2026-10-20; § Output) |
| 2 | Usage, argument, environment or migrations-history errors (steps 1 and 3; a usage error whose message cannot be written gives 1, or 130 for a Ctrl+C, decision D-4 option 1, and D-5 option a for an `OSError` that `argparse` would suppress); a source that cannot be built from the environment's proxy or certificate settings (step 2, F-03); `INVALID_TARGET` (a well-formed id that names no run, a `succeeded` run) |
| 130 | `INTERRUPTED` (including a `KeyboardInterrupt` during the final close of the repository, which keeps the confirmed status; Revision 9, F-22), or a `KeyboardInterrupt` that escapes `run_ingest` or interrupts steps 1 to 4 (for step 1, anywhere in `main()`, its parsing and `default_wiring()` included, through the handler of `main`; Revision 11, F-26, D-3 option B), or interrupts the closing of the source at step 7 (Revision 7, F-16), or interrupts the command while it writes its result at step 6 (Revision 10, sweep finding N-7; an exception other than `KeyboardInterrupt` that prevents writing the result gives 1, or keeps 130 when 130 was already determined, § Output) |

A `SystemExit` that does not come from `argparse` or from `main`'s own exits is not one of these codes: it is preserved and propagated through `run_ingest_command` and `main()` and the process exits with its own code, also when a `KeyboardInterrupt` arrived with it (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1; "`SystemExit`" in § Public names). Every code above reaches the process through `SystemExit` raised by `main` (CLI-3); Python's own exit code 120 (a failed exit-time flush) is never an outcome of `main` when the neutralizing of a failed stream succeeds (Revision 12, F-27); when the neutralizing fails, `main` still raises its agreed code, and only Python's finalization may replace it by 120 (D-10, a residual limitation). `--version` (0) and `argparse`'s usage errors (2) are unchanged with working streams; an `OSError` while `argparse` writes `--version`, `--help` or a usage message gives 1 (D-5 option a), a Ctrl+C there 130. A missing stream (`None`; Revision 13, audit finding F-31, D-11 and D-12) changes no code in this table: every code is the one the same invocation gives with that stream working (§ Public names, "A missing stream").

### Output (intent AC15)
- stdout:
  - exactly one line `<execution_id> <status>` when the outcome names a run, whatever the exit code, where `<status>` is the outcome's `run_status` value (`succeeded`, `failed` or `running`); the one exception is a `SystemExit`, after which no line is written ("`SystemExit`" in § Public names; Revision 11, F-24);
  - `<status>` is the last status this process confirmed for its run. `running` means that closing was not confirmed, not a claim that PostgreSQL still holds `running` (decision P-5);
  - nothing otherwise;
  - when any exception (a `KeyboardInterrupt` or any other) escapes `run_ingest` after `reporter.run_started(id)` was called, the line is `<id> running`, even if a `finish_run` may have committed unseen (decision P-5; extended from `KeyboardInterrupt` to every escaping exception in Revision 6, audit finding F-04); the exit code is 130 for a `KeyboardInterrupt` and 1 otherwise;
  - after the P-2 case of § State machine C, the line is `<id> succeeded` with exit 0;
  - at most one line per invocation, whatever happens after it is written: a `KeyboardInterrupt` while the source is closed (invocation step 7) adds no second line (Revision 7, audit finding F-16);
  - closing the final repository never changes the line: an `Exception` from that close is suppressed inside `run_ingest`, and a `KeyboardInterrupt` during it gives `INTERRUPTED` (exit 130) with the status already confirmed, so a run whose closing was confirmed prints `<id> succeeded` or `<id> failed`, never `<id> running` (Revision 9, audit finding F-22; § Public names, "The final close"); a `SystemExit` from that close propagates and no line is written (Revision 11, F-24).
- **Writing the result** (Revision 10, sweep finding N-7; required). The command writes its result at invocation step 6: after `run_ingest` returned, the final stderr message (`IngestOutcome.message`) and, when the outcome names a run, the result line with the outcome's `run_status`; after an exception escaped `run_ingest`, the handler's `<id> running` (when the run was announced) and its stderr line (`interrupted`, or `unexpected error: <class>`). A `KeyboardInterrupt` or any other exception (`MemoryError` included) raised while it writes any of these, by the stream or by a Ctrl+C, is handled by these rules:
  - *The confirmed status is kept.* Once `run_ingest` returned an outcome, the only result line the command attempts is the outcome's. An exception while it writes the final message or the result line never leads to `<id> running` or to any other status. In particular, a run confirmed `succeeded` never prints `<id> running` because writing failed. A failure while the final stderr message is written does not prevent the one attempt of the result line. The order of the two writes is free.
  - *At most one attempt.* The result line is attempted at most once per invocation. Once the command has started to write a result line (the outcome's line or a handler's `<id> running`), no other result line is attempted, whatever is raised afterwards, including a raise from the line end that follows the text. This extends F-16 (b).
  - *Exit code* (decided by the human on 2026-10-20, amending the architect's draft). A `KeyboardInterrupt` raised while the command writes gives exit 130. Any other exception (`OSError`, `MemoryError`, any `Exception`) that prevents writing the final result gives exit 1, unless an interruption had already made the exit code 130, which is kept (for example inside the command's handler for an escaping `KeyboardInterrupt`). In both cases the result line, if one is attempted, keeps the status above, the exception is suppressed, and PostgreSQL is not touched: the run is not read or modified because of it. So a run may stay `succeeded` in PostgreSQL while the command ends with 1 because of an output failure; the operator's evidence is the stored run, and the next `ingest` does not reconcile it (it is not `running`). A Ctrl+C is not swallowed: 130 records it (F-16 b, F-22).
  - *Nothing escapes.* No such exception escapes `run_ingest_command` (except `SystemExit`), so no traceback is printed. This holds also when the exception arrives inside the command's own handler for an exception that escaped `run_ingest`. The command may write a further stderr line about it (`interrupted`, or at most the class name); the failure of that line is suppressed in turn and gets no further line.
  - *`SystemExit`* (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1). A `SystemExit` raised while the command writes, alone or after a `KeyboardInterrupt` or another exception of an earlier write, is preserved and propagated: no further write is attempted and it escapes with its own code ("`SystemExit`" in § Public names).
  - *A usage error of invocation step 1* (Revision 11, audit finding F-26, decisions D-3 option B and D-4 option 1). Whether `argparse` or `run_ingest_command` writes it, the handler of `main` gives the same rules: one attempt, never repeated; 130 for a `KeyboardInterrupt`; 1 for any other exception (for an `OSError` that `argparse` would suppress, too: D-5 option a); a `SystemExit` preserved; stdout empty; no traceback.
  - *In a real process* (Revision 12, audit finding F-27, option (b)). Every write above is a protected write (§ Public names, "Protected writes and the last flush"): the text is written and flushed in the one attempt, so a failure that shows only when a buffered stream is flushed (a closed pipe) is a failure of that attempt, and, once the failed stream has been neutralized, Python's exit-time flush cannot fail afterwards (when the neutralizing fails, D-10). The outcome table there gives every case.
  - *A missing stream* (Revision 13, audit finding F-31, D-11 and D-12). A stream that is `None` is not a stream that fails: nothing is written on it or in its place, and none of the rules above about a write that fails applies (§ Public names, "A missing stream").
  - *Not guaranteed.* A complete line, when the output stream itself fails: the line being written (the result line or a stderr line) may be partial or missing, and nothing is written in its place. This is accepted.
- stderr:
  - `argparse` usage and errors;
  - the configuration message of invocation step 2;
  - the applied migration names;
  - `IngestReporter.progress` lines, at least the required ones of § Public names and signatures (F-07);
  - one final line with `IngestOutcome.message`;
  - for an exception that escapes, only its class name.
- Never on stderr or stdout: a traceback, an exception chain or `__context__` (R-6, raw-evidence N3), a URL, a header value, body bytes, a setting value or the password.
- The progress format is free; it names pages, sequences, statuses, content hashes, execution ids, operation names and event types, plus the fixed text or the class name of a project error as § Public names and signatures allows (the refused-write line of AC6 c adds at most the class name).

### Audit details (intent Q6, AC13; closed)
| Event type | Detail keys and types | Appended to |
|---|---|---|
| `run-started` | `resumes`: execution id `str` or `None`; `first_page`: `int` | the new run, right after `start_run` |
| `page-completed` | `page`: `int`; `sequence`: `int`; `content_hash`: 64-hex `str` | the run, after the page's observation is in both stores |
| `page-failed` | `page`: `int`; `sequence`: `int` or `None`; `failure`: `PageFailure`; `status`: `int` or `None` | the run |
| `storage-failed` | `page`: `int`; `sequence`: `int` or `None`; `store`: `EvidenceStoreName` | the run |
| `last-page-reached` | `page`: `int`; `pages`: `int` | the run |
| `run-interrupted` | `page`: `int` | the run |
| `unexpected-error` | `page`: `int`; `error_type`: class name `str` | the run |
| `observation-reconciled` | `sequence`: `int` | the run being reconciled |
| `run-closed-by-reconciliation` | `reconciled_observations`: `int` | the run being reconciled |
| `reconciliation-failed` | `sequence`: `int` or `None`; `problem`: `ReconciliationProblem` | the run being reconciled |

- Enum values are stored as their `str` value.
- `page` is the page being processed, or the first page the run would request when none was requested yet.
- `failure` comes from the error class: `SourceStatusError` -> `status` (with the response status); `IncompleteResponseError` -> `incomplete` (status 200); `SourceTransportError` -> `transport` (the partial response's status, or `None`).
- No detail holds a URL, a header value, body bytes, a setting or exception text.

### State machine P: one page (intent AC1 to AC6, AC17)
`next_sequence` starts at 1. Each page `p`, from the run's first page to request up to `scope.last_page`:

1. **Request.** `outcome = source.search_page(year=..., buyer=..., page=p)`. Catch `SourceError` only, and leave the `except` block before anything else (F2 consumer rules). `response = result.response` or `error.response`.
2. **No response** (`SourceTransportError` with `response is None`) -> `page-failed` (sequence `None`, `transport`, status `None`) -> step 7. No sequence is used (intent AC5).
3. **Store locally.** `observation = store.store(execution_id=..., sequence=next_sequence, **fields of response)`:
   - `OSError` or `EvidenceIntegrityError` -> ask `store.read_observation(id, next_sequence)`;
     - found -> `record_observation` of it (step 4, errors as there), then `storage-failed` (`raw-evidence`, sequence) -> step 7;
     - the read-back itself raises `EvidenceNotFoundError`, `OSError` or `EvidenceIntegrityError` -> treat the observation as not recorded: `storage-failed` (`raw-evidence`, page, sequence) -> step 7 (raw-evidence N2, intent AC6 a, b and d). The use case cannot tell whether a file was published and cannot record an observation it cannot read. This is not a claim that nothing was written. Nothing is lost: a resumption requests that page again, and an unreadable or orphan file counts for nothing (RE-9, intent AC9). For example, a regular file at `<root>/observations` makes the read-back raise `NotADirectoryError` (an `OSError`) on Linux and `EvidenceNotFoundError` on Windows; both end here.
     - `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit` and `MemoryError` from the read-back are not caught by this rule; they keep the handling of § State machine K (SA-11, Q11).
   - Once `store` returns, `next_sequence += 1`.
   - **Pending local observation** (State machine K; Revision 6, audit findings F-01 and F-02): sequence `s` is *pending* from the moment `store` is called for it until `record_observation` of that observation has returned, including its repeat under § State machine C. It stops being pending only when that `record_observation` returns, or when this step has concluded that the observation is not recorded (the failed read-back above). It is still pending while `store` runs, between `store` and `record_observation`, and while `record_observation` is in flight. It also stops being pending when that `record_observation` raises a refusal (a `PersistenceError` that is not a `PersistenceConnectionError`; Revision 10, sweep finding N-4; required). This holds for the record of step 4, for the record of a published observation after the read-back above, and for its repeat on the second repository under § State machine C. The pending period ends at the moment the refusal is raised, before its progress line is written. So a `KeyboardInterrupt`, a `MemoryError` or another exception that lands afterwards, while that line is written, while `storage-failed` is appended, or anywhere later, never makes § State machine K record that observation ("Refused writes" below: a refusal is answered, never retried).
4. **Record in PostgreSQL.** `record_observation(observation)`:
   - `PersistenceConnectionError` -> § State machine C with this operation pending;
   - any other `PersistenceError` -> a progress line naming `record_observation` and the identity (§ Public names and signatures, required progress 2), then `storage-failed` (`postgresql`, sequence) -> step 7.
5. **Decide.**
   - Error with a response -> `page-failed` (sequence, failure, status) -> step 7.
   - Result -> `page-completed` (page, sequence, content hash).
   - Then, if `p >= result.pages` -> `last-page-reached` (page `p`, `result.pages`) and finish `succeeded` (step 6). There is no condition on `scope.last_page` (decision P-3, intent AC3). The order is binding, and § State machine H validates against it: `page-completed` for `p`, then `last-page-reached` for the same `p`, then `finish_run`. This covers:
     - a beyond-the-last-page result (`is_beyond_last_page`, `p > pages`);
     - the reported last page itself (`p == pages`), also when it equals `scope.last_page`;
     - `pages` 0 with scope 1 to 1.

     No further request is sent, so AC17 (at most one request per page per run) is unaffected.
   - Otherwise (`p < result.pages`) go to the next page; after `scope.last_page`, step 6.
6. **End of range.** Once every page has been processed, `finish_run(SUCCEEDED, clock.now())`, keeping that `finished_at` value for any repeat.
7. **Failure path.** `finish_run(FAILED, clock.now())`, keeping that value, then `RUN_FAILED`. No further request is sent (no retries, Q12).

Every `append_audit_event` and `finish_run` above follows § State machine C on `PersistenceConnectionError`.

**Refused writes** (intent AC6 e, AC13; Revision 6, audit finding F-11). A *refusal* is a `PersistenceError` that is not a `PersistenceConnectionError` (`PersistenceIntegrityError`, `PersistenceNotFoundError` or a plain `PersistenceError`). Each refusal writes the required progress line, is never audited as `unexpected-error` and never enters § State machine K. Only catalogue values are used:

| Refused write | Reaction | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
