| `start_run` | nothing is written; `reporter.run_started` is not called | `NOT_STARTED`, no run (exit 1) |
| `record_observation` | step 4 | `RUN_FAILED`, `failed` |
| a normal-path append (`run-started`, `page-completed`, `last-page-reached`) | `storage-failed` (`postgresql`, the page, the sequence of the page's observation or `None`) once, then `finish_run(FAILED)` | `RUN_FAILED`, `failed` |
| the final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)` (step 6, or after `last-page-reached`) | `storage-failed` (`postgresql`, the last page processed, sequence `None`) once, then `finish_run(FAILED)` with a new clock reading | `RUN_FAILED`, `failed` |
| a failure-path append (`page-failed`, `storage-failed`) | no further event; `finish_run(FAILED)` (an exception that lands before that `finish_run` enters § State machine K, "After any other refusal"; Revision 11, F-23) | `RUN_FAILED`, `failed` |
| `finish_run(FAILED)` | nothing more is written; AC10 closes the run at the next `ingest` | `RUN_FAILED`, `running` (closing not confirmed, P-5) |

At most one `storage-failed` (`postgresql`) answers refusals in a run, so a refusal can never start a loop of events.

**A refusal is answered, never retried** (Revision 10, sweep finding N-4; required; it follows F-11). No write that the database refused is attempted again in the same invocation, by any path, § State machine K included:
- a refused `record_observation` ends the pending period of its sequence (P step 3), so K's step 2 never records that observation; K, entered for an exception that lands after the refusal, still runs its steps 1, 3 and 4 on the current repository (they are other writes);
- after a refused `finish_run(FAILED)` nothing more is written to the run by any path: an exception that lands afterwards (for example while the refusal's line is written) does not enter K, and the outcome is `INTERRUPTED` for a `KeyboardInterrupt` and `RUN_FAILED` otherwise, with `run_status=RUNNING` (stdout `<id> running`, exit 130 or 1);
- the refused write keeps its one progress line; a line interrupted while it is written may be missing or incomplete.

Inside § State machine C its own rules apply first (for example a refused repeat of the final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)`, step 5); a refusal of a write that C makes on its new connection follows this table.

### State machine C: after `PersistenceConnectionError` (intent AC8, Q3; PS-11, persistence Invariant 7)
Before the new run exists (the exclusion, the target check, reconciliation, chain checks, id draw), no new connection is opened: `NOT_STARTED`, exit 1. The lost operation gets the required progress line (§ Public names, required progress 3: the operation and the identity it concerns), and the outcome message is the fixed message of the state machine in which it happened (§ State machine T, R, H and I; Revision 7, audit findings F-14 and F-15), never only an exception's class name.

From `start_run` on, with pending operation `X` and its exact arguments kept:
1. Close the lost repository quietly. From the loss on it is no longer the current repository (see "The current repository" below): the record says so before the loss line is written and before this close. "Quietly" means (Revision 9, audit finding F-21; required): an `Exception` other than `MemoryError` raised by this `close()` is suppressed with no line, and the recovery continues with step 2; a `KeyboardInterrupt` or a `MemoryError` raised by it ends § State machine C in the window, and the final close of `run_ingest` attempts the lost repository once more (§ Public names, "The final close"). If this call already opened its second repository -> stop: the run is left `running`, `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status=RUNNING`.
2. Open the second repository through `open_repository()`. A failure -> left `running`; a `PersistenceError` here gets the required progress line naming `open_repository` (Revision 7). Any other exception here (Revision 8, audit finding F-19): "The current repository" below.
3. `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` on the second repository. `False` or an error, of any kind (Revision 8, audit finding F-18) -> close it; the run is not touched (no event, no `finish_run`); left `running`. An `Exception` other than `MemoryError` raised by that `close()` is suppressed and changes nothing; a `KeyboardInterrupt` or a `MemoryError` raised by it ends C in the window with the outcome of that exception (so `INTERRUPTED` for a `KeyboardInterrupt` that lands while the second repository is closed after a `False`), and the final close attempts the second repository once more (Revision 9, audit finding F-21). An error here gets the required progress line naming `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion` (Revision 7; for every kind of error, Revision 8). Only `True` makes the second repository the current one.

**The current repository** (Revision 8, audit findings F-18 and F-19; required). Every write and every read of the run after `start_run` goes through the *current* repository, and a repository is current only while it holds the ingest exclusion: the first repository until an operation on it raises `PersistenceConnectionError`; then no repository at all, until `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` on the second repository has returned `True`; then the second repository. The second repository never becomes current before that `True`.

**The explicit record** (Revision 9, audit finding F-21; required; it is how the rule above is kept, and it takes precedence over any routing of exceptions). The run session holds an explicit record of the current repository, whose value is either a repository that holds the ingest exclusion or *none*:
- it becomes *none* as the first action of the handler that catches a `PersistenceConnectionError` raised by an operation on the current repository, before the loss line is written and before step 1; this holds for the first loss and for a further loss on the second repository (step 6), and also before the run exists (where no new connection is opened);
- it becomes the second repository only right after that repository's `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` returned `True`;
- a lost repository, and a second repository that does not hold the exclusion, are kept only so that they can be closed; no other operation is ever called on them.

Every step of § State machine K and every read or write of the run requires a current repository, taken from this record when the exception is handled. With none, § State machine K is not entered, whatever the exception (a `KeyboardInterrupt`, a `MemoryError`, a synthetic defect, an error raised by the reporter, anything other than `SystemExit`) and wherever in the window it was raised: while the lost repository is closed, while the loss line is written, while `open_repository()` runs, while `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` runs, while a line of step 2 or 3 is written, while the second repository is closed after a `False` or an error, between the `True` and the update of the record, or between any of these. Which call raised, and whether the exception was wrapped or translated on its way, never decides it.

In the window with no current repository (steps 1 to 3):
- nothing reads or writes the run by any path, § State machine K included; no operation is attempted on the lost repository, which may have lost its session and with it the exclusion (PS-11), nor on a second repository that does not hold the exclusion;
- a `KeyboardInterrupt`, a `MemoryError` or any other exception that arrives in the window (anywhere in the list above), like a `PersistenceError` or a `False` at step 2 or 3, ends § State machine C: the run is not touched, § State machine K is not entered, and the second repository, if one was obtained, is closed (when that close is itself interrupted, the final close of `run_ingest` attempts it once more). The only exception that does not end C is an `Exception` other than `MemoryError` raised by a `close()` at step 1 or step 3, which is suppressed;
- the lines: when the exception came from one of the two database operations of the window, exactly one required progress line names it (`open_repository` or `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`, named alone, § Public names "Required progress"), with at most the exception's class name. When it landed anywhere else in the window (closing a repository, writing a line, between the steps), no further line is written for it. A `False` gets no line, and the closes of steps 1 and 3 are not database operations. A line interrupted while it is written may be missing or incomplete, and nothing is written in its place. No line names an operation that was not attempted (for example `read_run`), so no cleanup line is written;
- after `start_run` returned, the outcome is `INTERRUPTED` for a `KeyboardInterrupt`, and `RUN_FAILED` for a `False`, a `PersistenceError`, a `MemoryError` or any other `Exception`, always with the run's `execution_id` and `run_status=RUNNING` (closing not confirmed, P-5): stdout `<id> running`, exit 130 for Ctrl+C and 1 otherwise. AC10 closes the run at the next `ingest`;
- while `start_run` itself is the pending operation, the earlier rules give the outcome, unchanged: a `False` or a `PersistenceError` gives `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status=RUNNING` (stdout `<id> running`); a `KeyboardInterrupt` gives `INTERRUPTED` with no run (§ State machine K, "Before `start_run` returned"); any other `Exception` gives `NOT_STARTED` with no run and a message that is at most the class name. Nothing is written in any of them, and the line rule above applies;
- `SystemExit` propagates, and the second repository is still closed (§ Public names: `run_ingest` closes every repository it opened).
- **Two or more exceptions** (Revision 10, sweep finding N-3; required). More than one exception may arrive in the window, or in the closes that follow it: the closes of steps 1 and 3, and the final close's attempt once more of a repository whose close there was interrupted. Then precedence decides the outcome, whatever the order of arrival:
  - a `SystemExit` among them propagates, and the others are dropped; nothing is read or written, § State machine K is not entered, and every repository is still attempted (Revision 11, audit finding F-24, decision D-1; "`SystemExit`" in § Public names);
  - otherwise, a `KeyboardInterrupt` among them gives the `KeyboardInterrupt` outcome: `INTERRUPTED`, exit 130, with the run and `run_status=RUNNING` after `start_run` returned, or with no run while `start_run` is pending;
  - otherwise, any other exception that ends C (a `MemoryError`, a defect) gives its outcome: `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status=RUNNING` after `start_run` returned, or `NOT_STARTED` with no run while `start_run` is pending, exit 1. It also prevails over a `False` or a `PersistenceError` of step 2 or 3, as step 3 already says for its close;
  - an `Exception` other than `MemoryError` from a close stays suppressed and takes no part.

  In every case nothing is read or written, § State machine K is not entered, and the lines are those of the database operation that raised (none for a close). This agrees with "The final close" (F-22): a `KeyboardInterrupt` there turns the determined outcome into `INTERRUPTED`, and an `Exception` there is suppressed.

A `KeyboardInterrupt` that lands after the `True` and before the second repository was made current is in the window too: nothing is written and the second repository is closed. Writing nothing is always safe; writing without the exclusion never is (Q4).
4. Resolve `X`:

   | `X` | Safe to repeat | Resolution |
   |---|---|---|
   | `start_run`, `record_observation`, `finish_run` | yes | repeat with the same values (same scope and `started_at`; same `Observation`; same status and `finished_at`) |
   | `read_run`, `read_observation`, `list_*` | yes (reads) | repeat |
   | `append_audit_event(type, detail, occurred_at)` | **no** | `list_audit_events(id)`; if an event with the same type, a `same_json` detail and the same `occurred_at` instant exists, treat it as stored; otherwise append once |

5. Continue the path that was interrupted:
   - on the normal path (P steps 1 to 6, or the opening `start_run` and `run-started`), append `storage-failed` (`postgresql`, page, sequence or `None`), then `finish_run(FAILED)`;
   - on a failure path, complete that path.
   - Exception (decision P-2, intent AC8): if `X` was the final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)`, it is repeated with the same `finished_at`. No `storage-failed` is appended and no `finish_run(FAILED)` is attempted.
     - When the repeat returns the run with status `succeeded` and that `finished_at`, the run is **confirmed** `succeeded`: `SUCCEEDED`, exit 0. This holds whether the first attempt had committed or not: F3 returns the stored run for a repeat with the same status and instant, finishes a still-`running` run, and raises `PersistenceIntegrityError` for anything else (PS-7).
     - Confirmation is that successful repeat only. A separate `read_run` is not used: it would add a second path that still needs the repeat when the run is `running`.
     - A `PersistenceIntegrityError` from the repeat, which cannot happen under the exclusion, means `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status` from the stored run (exit 1). The refused repeat gets the required progress line (`finish_run` `succeeded`, the run). When the `read_run` that reads the stored run is itself refused or lost, it gets its own required progress line (`read_run`, the run; Revision 7, audit finding F-13), nothing more is written, no third connection is opened, and the outcome is `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status=RUNNING`.
     - If step 2 or 3 failed, or C ended earlier, the repeat is never made: left `running`, exit 1. The exit code is 130 when a Ctrl+C ended C anywhere in the window: at step 2 or 3 ("The current repository" above, Revision 8), and also while the lost repository was closed at step 1 or while the loss line was written (Revision 10, sweep finding N-8; the window list of "The explicit record").
6. Any further `PersistenceConnectionError` -> left `running`; no third connection. The record becomes *none* again (Revision 9, F-21), so an exception that lands afterwards (for example while the line of the second loss is written) does not enter § State machine K either: `INTERRUPTED` for a `KeyboardInterrupt` and `RUN_FAILED` otherwise, with `run_status=RUNNING`. When the operation lost the second time is the repeat of `start_run` on the second repository, the outcomes of the `start_run` window (the last bullets of "The current repository" above) apply instead (Revision 10, sweep finding N-1; required): the second loss itself gives `RUN_FAILED` with `run_status=RUNNING` (stdout `<id> running`, as a `PersistenceError` there does). A `KeyboardInterrupt` that lands afterwards, before C has determined the outcome (for example while the line of the second loss is written), gives `INTERRUPTED` with no run. Any other `Exception` there gives `NOT_STARTED` with no run and a message that is at most the class name. In the last two, nothing is written, stdout is empty, `reporter.run_started` is not called, and no third connection is opened. Once the outcome is determined, the final close follows § Public names, "The final close".

`started_at`, `finished_at` and `occurred_at` are read from the clock once and reused for every repeat, so that "exactly the same values" holds.

### State machine K: interruption and unexpected exceptions (intent AC7, AC11, AC15, Q11; best effort)
- **Only through the current repository** (Revision 8, audit findings F-18 and F-19; required; it takes precedence over "the same steps 1 to 4" below). The cleanup runs only on the current repository of § State machine C, which is one that holds the ingest exclusion. When the `KeyboardInterrupt` or the other exception arrives while there is no current repository (C steps 1 to 3, after a lost connection and before the second repository's exclusion returned `True`), the cleanup does not run: no step below is taken, nothing is read from or written to the run, and § State machine C, "The current repository", gives the lines and the outcome (`INTERRUPTED` or `RUN_FAILED`, `run_status=RUNNING`). Whether a current repository exists is read from the explicit record of § State machine C when the exception is handled (Revision 9, audit finding F-21): with none, K is not entered, whatever the exception and wherever in the window it was raised, and no line names an operation of K.
- `KeyboardInterrupt` raised inside the run, after `start_run` returned. The cleanup, in this order:
  1. **Is the run still open?** (Revision 6, audit finding F-05, intent AC11.) `read_run(id)` on the current repository. If the run is `succeeded` or `failed` (the interrupted operation was a `finish_run` that had committed), the cleanup writes nothing: no observation, no event, no `finish_run`. It returns `INTERRUPTED` with `run_status` = the stored status. If the run is `running`, continue.
  2. **Pending local observation** (P step 3; F-01, F-02). If a sequence `s` is pending (a refused `record_observation` of `s` already ended its pending period, so a refused record is never attempted again here; Revision 10, sweep finding N-4), ask `store.read_observation(id, s)`:
     - found -> `record_observation` of it;
     - `EvidenceNotFoundError`, `EvidenceIntegrityError` or `OSError` -> nothing was published, or what was published cannot be read: record nothing for `s` and continue with step 3 (the rule of F-3 and intent AC6 d; nothing is lost, because a resumption requests that page again and an orphan or unreadable file counts for nothing).
  3. Append `run-interrupted` (page).
  4. `finish_run(FAILED)`.
  5. Return `INTERRUPTED` with the `run_status` that `finish_run` returned.
- Any other `Exception` that is not a project error (a synthetic transport defect, `MemoryError`), raised inside the run after `start_run` returned, while a current repository exists (first bullet): the same steps 1 to 4, with `unexpected-error` (page, class name) at step 3, returning `RUN_FAILED`. At step 1, a run already finished gives `RUN_FAILED` with the stored status (exit 1).
- What the cleanup tolerates (best effort, Q11):
  - It opens no new connection (no § State machine C), and it never uses a repository that is not the current one (first bullet).
  - The three read-back errors of step 2 are the only exceptions it absorbs and continues past.
  - Any other `Exception` raised during the cleanup stops it at once: a `PersistenceError` from `read_run`, `record_observation`, the append or `finish_run`, a `MemoryError`, or anything else. Nothing more is written, and it returns the outcome of its kind (`INTERRUPTED` or `RUN_FAILED`) with `run_status=RUNNING` (closing not confirmed, P-5). In particular, when `read_run` itself fails, nothing is written, because the cleanup cannot show that the run is still open (AC11); AC10 closes the run at the next `ingest`.
  - **Progress line** (Revision 7, audit finding F-13; required progress 2 and 3): whenever one of the cleanup's own database operations, the step 1 `read_run`, the step 2 `record_observation` of the pending observation, the step 3 append (`run-interrupted` or `unexpected-error`) or the step 4 `finish_run(FAILED)`, raises an `Exception` (refused, lost, or anything else), the cleanup writes exactly one `reporter.progress` line before it stops: fixed words, the operation (with the event type for the append, the sequence `s` for the record, `failed` for `finish_run`), the run's execution id, and at most the exception's class name. Then it returns as above. The three read-back errors of step 2 come from the store, not the database, and need no line. A `KeyboardInterrupt` during the cleanup propagates without a required line.
  - **Any `KeyboardInterrupt` raised during the cleanup propagates out of `run_ingest`** (Revision 10, sweep finding N-2; required), whether the cleanup was entered for a `KeyboardInterrupt` (a second one) or for a `MemoryError` or another unexpected exception, and wherever in the cleanup it lands, including after the step 1 `read_run` returned and while the cleanup writes the line of a failed operation. `SystemExit` propagates too. The final close is still attempted (§ Public names, "The final close", last bullet), and the command treats the interrupt as any escaping `KeyboardInterrupt` (invocation step 6): exit 130, stdout `<id> running` (closing not confirmed, P-5), stderr `interrupted`, no traceback. Chosen because it is the approved behavior of a second Ctrl+C during the cleanup (AC7, the `interrupt` row) and of P-5, and the code already behaves this way for both kinds of cleanup. F-05, F-16 (b) and F-22 keep a status only when it was already confirmed and nothing was in flight; during K a write may be in flight, so `running` is the honest line.
- Before `start_run` returned, there is nothing to clean: `INTERRUPTED` with no run. If `start_run` committed unseen, AC10 closes the run later.
- A database refusal never reaches this machine as an "unexpected" error: a `PersistenceError` raised by a write of P is handled by P step 4, § Refused writes or § State machine C, and is never audited as `unexpected-error` (F-11). After a refused `finish_run(FAILED)` the cleanup does not run at all, and a refused `record_observation` is never recorded by step 2 (§ State machine P, "A refusal is answered, never retried"; Revision 10, sweep finding N-4).
- **After any other refusal** (Revision 11, audit finding F-23, decision D-2 option 1, chosen by the human on 2026-10-21, which is the current behavior; no code change). After a refused `page-failed` or `storage-failed` append, a refused normal-path append (`run-started`, `page-completed`, `last-page-reached`) or a refused final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)`, a `KeyboardInterrupt`, a `MemoryError` or another exception that lands before the run is closed (for example while the refusal's line is written, before the answer of the "Refused writes" table was made) enters this machine on the current repository like any other: step 1 `read_run`; step 2 only for a sequence still pending (none after a refused record); step 3 `run-interrupted` (or `unexpected-error`) for the page being processed; step 4 `finish_run(FAILED)`. These are other writes: the refused write is never attempted again and the refused event is never appended by K. When K's own append or `finish_run` is refused in turn, K writes its line and stops (the run stays `running`, "What the cleanup tolerates").
- `SystemExit` is preserved and propagated, and it prevails over any other exception (§ Public names, "`SystemExit`"; Revision 11, F-24; until Revision 10: "is never caught").

### State machine T: resumption target (intent AC9, AC14; finding 22)
`read_run(target)` gives one of:
- `PersistenceNotFoundError` -> `INVALID_TARGET`;
- `SUCCEEDED` -> `INVALID_TARGET`;
- `RUNNING` or `FAILED` -> continue;
- any other `PersistenceError`, a refusal or a `PersistenceConnectionError` (Revision 7, audit finding F-15) -> `NOT_STARTED`, never `INVALID_TARGET` (exit 1, not 2: the target's existence is unknown), no new connection, the required progress line (`read_run`, the target id), and a fixed outcome message naming the target id that does not call it invalid (suggested: `the resumption target <id> could not be read`).

Nothing is written in this step.

### State machine R: reconciliation (intent AC10, Q4, Q14)
For each run `X` in `list_runs(RUNNING)` (in its order), while the exclusion is held:
1. `local = store.list_observation_sequences(X)`; `EvidenceIntegrityError` or any `OSError` -> problem `local-listing-failed` (sequence `None`).
2. `pg = {o.sequence: o for o in repository.list_observations(X)}`.
3. For each sequence in `sorted(set(local) | set(pg))`, the first problem found ends the step:
   - in `pg` but not `local` -> `local-observation-missing`;
   - `store.read_observation(X, s)` raises `EvidenceIntegrityError`, `EvidenceNotFoundError` (the file was listed and vanished before it was read) or any `OSError` (for example `PermissionError`) -> `local-observation-unreadable` (Revision 6, audit finding F-10);
   - in `pg` and different from it -> `local-observation-different`;
   - `store.read_content(o.content_hash)` raises `EvidenceNotFoundError` -> `object-missing`; `EvidenceIntegrityError` or any `OSError` -> `object-integrity` (the object cannot be shown to match its hash; Revision 6, F-10).
4. After every listed sequence is verified, the union must be exactly `1..max`; the smallest missing number (or a `0`) -> `sequence-gap` with that sequence. A later observation is verified before the gap is reported (intent AC10).
5. For each local-only sequence, in order: `record_observation(o)`; a refusal (`PersistenceIntegrityError`, or any other `PersistenceError` that is not a connection loss) -> `postgresql-observation-conflict`; then `observation-reconciled` (sequence).
6. `run-closed-by-reconciliation` (count of step 5), then `finish_run(X, FAILED, clock.now())`.

On a problem: `reconciliation-failed` (sequence, problem), `finish_run(X, FAILED)`, `NOT_STARTED`. Runs not yet examined stay `running` for the next invocation. Orphan objects are never looked for. A `PersistenceConnectionError` here (from `list_runs`, `list_observations(X)`, a `record_observation` of `X`, an append to `X` or `finish_run(X, FAILED)`) -> `NOT_STARTED`, with no new connection; nothing more is written to `X`, which may stay `running` for the next invocation; the required progress line names the lost operation and `X` (`list_runs` is named alone); and the outcome message is the same fixed message as a refusal below, naming `X` (or saying that the running runs could not be listed), never only an exception's class name (Revision 7, audit findings F-14 and F-15).

The closed list of Q6 is not extended (F-10): an `OSError` or a vanished file is always mapped to the closest value above, so it never escapes the reconciliation: it is recorded as `reconciliation-failed` and `X` is finished `failed`, with exit 1 and no new run, unless that write itself is refused or lost (next sentence and the connection rule above). A refusal (F-11) of `list_runs` or `list_observations`, of an append (`observation-reconciled`, `run-closed-by-reconciliation`, `reconciliation-failed`) or of `finish_run(X, FAILED)` -> nothing more is written to `X`, the required progress line names the operation and `X`, and the outcome is `NOT_STARTED` with a message naming `X`; `X` may stay `running` for the next invocation.

### State machine H: resumption chain (intent AC9, AC24, Q13, Q14)
1. Starting at the target, follow `run-started.resumes` to a run whose `resumes` is `None`. For each run:
   - not found -> malformed;
   - already visited -> cyclic;
   - not exactly one `run-started` event, or its detail not exactly `{"resumes": str | None, "first_page": int >= 1}` (a `bool` rejected, `resumes` passing `validate_execution_id`) -> malformed;
   - scope not readable by `IngestScope.from_json`, or not `same_json` to the target's scope -> contradictory;
   - a run other than the target that is `succeeded` or `running` -> contradictory.
2. For every `page-completed` event in the chain, the detail must have exactly `page`, `sequence` and `content_hash`, with the page inside the scope range. That run's observation for that sequence must have status 200 and that content hash both in PostgreSQL (`read_observation`) and in the store (`read_observation`); otherwise -> contradictory. A read that fails counts as "otherwise" (Revision 6, audit finding F-06): `PersistenceNotFoundError` from PostgreSQL, and `EvidenceNotFoundError`, `EvidenceIntegrityError` or any `OSError` from the store (a local record that is missing, corrupt or unreadable), each -> contradictory, with the fixed message below.
3. **Validation of `last-page-reached`** (required by the human on 2026-10-15, intent AC24). Event order is the order `list_audit_events` returns (by `event_id`). Before any such event is used as a limit, every one in the chain must pass all of these, or the chain is malformed:
   - its detail has exactly the keys `page` and `pages`, each an `int` that is not a `bool` (floats cannot be stored, PS-10);
   - `scope.first_page <= page <= scope.last_page` (the chain shares one scope, step 1), `pages >= 0` and `page >= pages`;
   - it is the only `last-page-reached` event of its run, and the only one in the whole chain. Two events, in one run or in two, are rejected rather than ranked: no precedence rule exists (confirmed by the human on 2026-10-15), and the consequence below shows none is needed;
   - the same run holds a `page-completed` event for the same `page`, earlier in its event order, that passed step 2. This is the order State machine P writes (P step 5);
   - no `page-completed` and no `page-failed` event of that run comes after it in its event order. Other events may follow it: `run-interrupted`, `unexpected-error`, `storage-failed` (an interrupted or failed close; confirmed by the human on 2026-10-15), and the reconciliation events;
   - **coverage** (required by the human on 2026-10-15): at the point where the event for page `p` is reached, the set `covered` contains every page from `scope.first_page` to `p`. `scope` is the one scope the whole chain shares (step 1, `same_json`), not a run's `run-started.first_page`.
     - `covered` is built in one deterministic walk: runs from the oldest (`resumes` `None`) to the resumed target, and within each run its events in `list_audit_events` order.
     - Each `page-completed` that passed step 2 adds its page. Step 2 is the existing definition of a valid `page-completed`: a page inside `[scope.first_page, scope.last_page]` and the sequence of a `200` observation present in both stores with the same content hash (intent AC1, AC5, Q14).
     - A `page-completed` for a page below `scope.first_page` or above `scope.last_page` never reaches `covered`: step 2 already makes the chain malformed.
     - Any page of `[scope.first_page, p]` missing from `covered` -> malformed.

   **Consequence** (used by steps 4 and 5): after a valid event for page `p`, every page of `[scope.first_page, p]` is in `covered` and no page above `p` is left to request, so nothing is pending. Step 5 then forces every later run of the chain to be a sentinel run (`first_page = last_page + 1`, no page events). A sentinel run holds no `page-completed`, so it cannot hold the preceding `page-completed` that a second valid event needs; and a second event earlier in the chain would make the run that wrote this one a sentinel run too. So two events are never both valid, and a second one is always malformed.

   The event that passes is *the chain's valid `last-page-reached`*; its `page` is the `last_reached` of `pages_left_to_request`. An event that fails never limits anything: the resumption ends `NOT_STARTED` (exit 1) before any request.

   Interaction with reconciliation (§ State machine R): the order is fixed. R runs for every `running` run (invocation step 5.4) before this step (5.5). R reads observations only, never audit events, and appends only `observation-reconciled`, `run-closed-by-reconciliation` or `reconciliation-failed`, none of which is a page event. In particular, R never appends a `page-completed` (Q14: completeness is never decided from stored bytes), so `covered` holds only the `page-completed` events the runs wrote themselves. A run closed by R is validated here like any other, and R can neither create nor repair a `last-page-reached`.

   Interaction with the sentinel (P-4, step 5): a run whose `first_page` is `last_page + 1` holds no page events, so it adds nothing to `covered` and cannot hold a `last-page-reached`. Its own `first_page` is accepted only when step 4, applied to the chain before it, finds nothing left; a valid event earlier in the chain is one way that happens.
4. *Left to request*: a page of `[first_page, last_page]` that no run in the chain completed and that is not above the `page` of the chain's valid `last-page-reached` (step 3; intent AC3 across a chain).
   - The new run requests, in increasing order, every page left to request.
   - Its `run-started` gets `resumes` = target and `first_page` = the smallest page left to request.
   - When none is left, `first_page` is `last_page + 1` (decision P-4): a sentinel valid only for that case. The run then requests nothing and finishes `succeeded`.
5. Validation of every `first_page` already in the chain (decision P-4, intent AC24), applied to each run in step 1, after step 3:
   - a run with `resumes` `None` must have `first_page == scope.first_page`;
   - a run with `resumes` = R must have `first_page` equal to what step 4 computes from the chain that starts at R. That is the smallest page left to request after R's chain, counting R's chain's valid `last-page-reached` if any, or `last_page + 1` exactly when none was left.
   - Any other value is malformed, inside the range or out of it (for example `0`, `last_page + 2`, or `last_page + 1` while a page was still left).
   - A run whose `first_page` is `last_page + 1` must hold no `page-completed` and no `page-failed` event; otherwise it is malformed.

   The same domain function both computes and validates, so the check is pure: `domain/ingestion_catalogue.py` gains `pages_left_to_request(scope: IngestScope, completed: frozenset[int], last_reached: int | None) -> tuple[int, ...]`.

Any failure -> `NOT_STARTED`, with a fixed message naming the execution id; nothing is appended to any run (Q5; reconciliation events already written by R stay). The message holds fixed words and the target execution id (it may also name the run of the chain concerned); it is never only an exception's class name and never `str()` of an exception that is not a project error (intent AC24; F-06).

A database read of this machine (`read_run` of a run of the chain, `list_audit_events`, PostgreSQL `read_observation`) that raises a `PersistenceError` other than `PersistenceNotFoundError`, a refusal or a `PersistenceConnectionError`, is an operational failure, not a verdict on the chain (Revision 7, audit finding F-15): `NOT_STARTED` (exit 1), no new connection, nothing appended to any run, the required progress line (the operation and the run read), and a fixed message that names the target execution id and does not call the chain invalid, malformed, cyclic or contradictory (suggested: `the resumption chain of <id> could not be read`). `PersistenceNotFoundError` keeps the meaning steps 1 and 2 give it.

### State machine I: new execution id (intent AC12, Q7)
Up to three draws:
- `id = execution_ids.new_execution_id(started_at)`;
- `validate_ingest_execution_id(id)` (`ValueError` -> `NOT_STARTED`);
- `read_run(id)` must raise `PersistenceNotFoundError`;
- `store.list_observation_sequences(id)` must be `()`.

After three taken ids -> `NOT_STARTED`. The check and `start_run` happen under the exclusion. Sequences start at 1, with no gaps (P step 3).

A check that cannot be made (Revision 7, audit finding F-15): `read_run(id)` raising a `PersistenceError` other than `PersistenceNotFoundError` (a refusal, or a `PersistenceConnectionError`), or `store.list_observation_sequences(id)` raising `OSError` or `EvidenceIntegrityError` -> `NOT_STARTED` (exit 1), no `start_run`, no new connection, nothing written, and a fixed message naming that id (suggested: `the execution id <id> could not be checked`). The database case also gets the required progress line (`read_run`, that id).

### F1 port extension: `list_observation_sequences` (intent AC22, Q14)
- Returns the sequence numbers of the files `observations/<execution_id>/<n>.json` under the root, as a sorted tuple, where `<n>` is the canonical decimal form of a non-negative integer (no sign, no leading zero except `0` itself). It does not stop at a gap.
- `ValueError` for an id that fails `validate_execution_id`, before any file system access.
- `()` when `observations/<execution_id>` does not exist; nothing is created.
- `EvidenceIntegrityError` naming the execution, and no content, for:
  - an entry of that directory that is not a regular file named `<n>.json` (`x.json`, `01.json`, a subdirectory);
  - a non-directory at that path.
- `OSError` from listing propagates unwrapped (RE-7).
- It reads no file contents, follows the existing layout only, never lists `tmp/` or `objects/`, and writes, renames or deletes nothing.
- Unchanged guarantees: RE-1 to RE-11, write-once publication, no deletion, integrity on read (RE-7, RE-8, RE-10), and `store`, `read_content` and `read_observation` byte for byte.

### F3 port extension: `list_runs` and the ingest exclusion (intent AC23, Q4)
- `list_runs(status)`:
  - `ValueError` before any SQL when `status` is not a `RunStatus`;
  - otherwise one read transaction, ordered by `started_at` then `execution_id`, `()` when there is none;
  - rows are mapped with the existing `_run_from_row`;
  - errors as PS-11 and PS-12.
- `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()`:
  - a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock(INGEST_EXCLUSION_KEY)` on the repository's own connection (never the `_xact_` form, never the waiting `pg_advisory_lock`), committed as one operation; it writes no row;
  - `True` when acquired or already held by this repository (a flag prevents a second, stacked acquisition), `False` when another session holds it;
  - held until `close()` or the end of the session, with no release operation;
  - a lost connection -> `PersistenceConnectionError`, and a call after `close()` -> `RuntimeError`, both as PS-11;
  - independent of `MIGRATION_LOCK_KEY` (PS-4);
  - the key, the SQL and the words of the PostgreSQL mechanism never appear in a message, `repr` or `args` (PS-12).
- Unchanged: the eight existing operations, their transactions, their errors, and the "one thread at a time" docstring.

### No schema change
F4 adds no migration file: `migrations/` keeps `0001_initial_schema.sql` only (intent AC20).
- The exclusion is session state of PostgreSQL, not schema.
- `list_runs` reads existing columns. The runs table is small for a bounded batch CLI, so no index on `status` is added; a later migration can add one if it is ever measured as needed.
- The catalogue is enforced by the use case and the domain enums, not by a `CHECK` on `event_type`, which would need a new migration and make the catalogue a schema change.

### Port types keep satisfying their adapters
`LocalDiskRawEvidenceStore` implements `list_observation_sequences`, and `PostgresIngestionRepository` implements `list_runs` and `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`, in the same change that extends each port. So the existing annotated assignments keep passing `mypy` unchanged:
- `tests/unit/test_local_disk_raw_evidence_store.py`: `LocalDiskRawEvidenceStore` assigned to `RawEvidenceStore`;
- the F3 `satisfies_port` test: `PostgresIngestionRepository` assigned to `IngestionRepository`.

No other class implements either protocol today (checked with `grep` on 2026-10-14). The in-memory doubles in `tests/unit/ingest/` implement the extended ports from the start.

### Tests
- **Tiers.** Unit tests (`tests/unit/`) use in-memory doubles, or a `tmp_path` store for F1, and open no socket. Integration tests (`tests/integration/`, marker `integration`) use throwaway databases exactly as PT-13 (`migrated_database`, `empty_database`, the admin connection), test stores on `tmp_path`, and skip or fail by policy. CLI tests call `run_ingest_command` with an injected wiring, or `main([...])` with `default_wiring` replaced through `monkeypatch`. Process tests (`tests/process/`, Revision 12, audit finding F-27, authorized by the human on 2026-10-22) run the real `cli.main` in a child Python process started by the test, with stub collaborators set up by a bootstrap script the test writes into `tmp_path`; they open no socket, touch no database and no real `data/raw/` (§ Validation, "Process tier").
- **Network guard.**
  - `tests/unit/ingest/conftest.py` holds an `autouse` guard like F2's (it patches `socket.socket.connect`, `connect_ex`, `socket.create_connection` and `socket.getaddrinfo`).
  - `tests/integration/ingest/conftest.py` holds the same guard, except that `getaddrinfo` is allowed for the configured `POSTGRES_HOST` only. `libpq` opens its own sockets outside Python (§ Guidance consulted), so PostgreSQL works while any `httpx` attempt fails.
  - F2's guard and file are not touched.
  - Source URLs use `https://source.invalid/PLATAFORMA`.
- **No real `data/raw/`.**
  - Every test store lives on `tmp_path`.
  - AC25 uses a synthetic repository root `<T>` under `tmp_path`, holding a copy of the repository's `migrations/` files, entered with `monkeypatch.chdir`.
  - A session fixture in `tests/integration/ingest/conftest.py` records the names, sizes and modification times under the repository's real `data/raw/` at session start and asserts that they are unchanged at session end.
- **Fault-injection seams**, all in test code:

  | Seam | Supplied by | Used for |
  |---|---|---|
  | `KeyboardInterrupt`, `MemoryError` or a synthetic exception while page `p` is requested | the `MockTransport` handler (SA-11: propagates unchanged) or a `FakeSource` double | AC7, AC15 |

Integration: `KillBeforeFinishRepository`, wrapping the real adapter; on `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)` it first calls `pg_terminate_backend` for its own backend through the admin connection, then delegates (the delegate raises `PersistenceConnectionError`; nothing was written) | AC8 (P-2) |
  | P-2, after commit: the final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)` committed, then the reply was lost | unit: `FlakyRepository` delegating, then raising `PersistenceConnectionError`. Integration: `KillAfterFinishRepository`, wrapping the real adapter; it delegates (the commit happens), then terminates its own backend and raises `PersistenceConnectionError` itself, standing in for the reply lost on the wire (persistence U1: the real commit-in-flight loss is not deterministic) | AC8 (P-2) |

- **Data.** Synthetic only: buyer `SYNTHETIC BUYER S.A.`, page bodies built in the observed shape or read from `tests/fixtures/sercop/`, markers `synthetic-password-marker`, `synthetic-body-marker` and `synthetic-header-marker`. JSON details and scopes are compared with `same_json`.

### Other constraints
- No dependency is added (`AGENTS.md`, PT-11, intent AC18): `uv.lock` is unchanged, and `pyproject.toml` changes only by the two `[tool.mypy]` keys of decision F-1 and their comment (§ Ownership).
- One `SercopSource` per invocation, built by `interfaces` and closed in a `finally`. At most two repositories per invocation, opened by `run_ingest`. One thread. No injected callable calls back into the source (SA-13, R-11, PS-13).
- `SercopSource` is built with the wiring clock's `now`, so `captured_at` comes from the one clock (intent AC12).

## Proposed approach
Revisable by the executor if it records why, except where a passage cites a gate 1 decision (Qn) or a finding: only the human changes those.
- Decision: the use case opens the repository through `RepositoryOpener` — rationale: Q3 needs exactly one new connection created by the code that knows which operation is pending; `interfaces` still decides which adapter is built (intent AC18); rejected: `interfaces` passing a ready repository plus a reconnect callback (two objects for one concern).
- Decision: `IngestReporter` instead of `print` in `application` — rationale: `application` holds no I/O, and `interfaces` needs to learn the run id before the outcome so it can print `<id> running` after a second Ctrl+C; rejected: returning the id only in `IngestOutcome` (lost when the cleanup is interrupted).
- Decision: the listing returns sequence numbers, not observations — rationale: a corrupt record is reported per sequence by `read_observation` and never hides later ones (Q14, intent AC22); rejected: returning `Observation` objects (the first corrupt file would stop the listing).
- Decision (decision P-3 of the human, 2026-10-15; not revisable by the executor): `last-page-reached` is appended whenever the completed page `p` satisfies `p >= result.pages`, with no condition on `scope.last_page`, after that page's `page-completed`, and the run then ends `succeeded` — rationale: the event records that the source has no page after `p`, which is true whatever the scope says, and a resumption relies on it (intent AC3, AC24); rejected: the Revision 1 rule, which added a condition on `scope.last_page` (the no-results scope 1 to 1 would end without the event).
- Decision: the gap check runs after every listed sequence is verified — rationale: intent AC10 says a later observation is verified and not hidden by the gap.
- Decision: reconciliation stops at the first run with a problem — rationale: a problem means a human must look; continuing would add closures that hide the order of events; the remaining `running` runs are reconciled by the next invocation.
- Decision: the CLI keeps its parser in `cli.py` and delegates to `ingest_command.py` — rationale: CLI-3 stays where it is, and `cli.py` stays thin.
- Suggestion: `tests/unit/ingest/conftest.py` provides `InMemoryRawEvidenceStore` (keeps objects and observations in dicts and applies F1's `Observation` validation), `InMemoryIngestionRepository` (applies the same identity and idempotency rules as PS-7 to PS-9, models the exclusion with a shared flag), `FakeSource`, `FixedClock`, `ScriptedExecutionIds`, `RecordingReporter` and the wrappers of the seams table.
- Suggestion: suggested test files and `-k` tokens:
  - `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingestion_catalogue.py`: `catalogue`, `scope`, `execution_id`;
  - `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_use_case.py`: `success`, `repeat`, `last_page`, `failure_with_response`, `failure_without_response`, `storage`, `interrupt`, `connection_loss`, `resume`, `reconcile`, `closed_run`, `identity`, `chain`, `no_retry`, `unexpected`;
  - `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_command.py`: `usage`, `environment`, `exit_code`, `output`, `logger`, `paths`;
  - `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_layers.py`: `layer`;
  - `tests/unit/test_local_disk_raw_evidence_store_listing.py`: `listing`;
  - `tests/integration/ingest/test_postgres_runs_and_exclusion.py`: `list_runs`, `exclusion`;
  - `tests/integration/ingest/test_ingest_end_to_end.py`: `e2e_`;
  - `tests/integration/ingest/test_ingest_connection_loss.py`: `connection_loss`;

  - the validation of `last-page-reached`: `chain_last_page` in `tests/unit/ingest/test_ingest_use_case.py`, and `e2e_chain_last_page` in `tests/integration/ingest/test_ingest_end_to_end.py`;

### Decisions (decided by the human on 2026-10-15)
The five findings of Revision 1 are closed; intent Revision 4 § Decisions on the execution-plan findings records them.

- **P-1, accepted.** `MigrationLockError(MigrationError)` in `domain/persistence_errors.py`, raised by `apply_migrations` exclusively for a busy migration lock, with the same fixed message (§ Public names and signatures). `interfaces` maps it to exit 1 and any other `MigrationError` to exit 2 (§ Order of an invocation, step 3).
- **P-2, accepted, with a contract change to intent AC8.** A run is confirmed `succeeded` only by the successful exact repeat of its final `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)` on the one new connection: exit 0. Every other connection loss exits 1 (§ State machine C, step 5).
- **P-3, the Revision 1 reading not accepted.** `last-page-reached` is appended whenever `p >= result.pages`, with no condition on `scope.last_page`, and the run ends `succeeded` (§ State machine P, step 5). Pages above the chain's valid `last-page-reached` are never requested, also across a resumption chain (§ State machine H, steps 3 and 4).
- **P-4, accepted.** `first_page = last_page + 1` only for a resumption with nothing left to request; `pages_left_to_request` validates every `first_page` of a chain, so any other unjustified value is a malformed chain (§ State machine H, steps 4 and 5).
- **P-5, accepted.** stdout `<id> running` after an interrupted cleanup means "last status confirmed by the process; closing not confirmed" (§ Output).
- **Hierarchy test, confirmed by the human on 2026-10-15.** The existing direct-subclass test in `tests/unit/test_ingestion_audit.py` is not edited. The new `tests/unit/test_persistence_error_hierarchy.py` pins `MigrationLockError.__bases__ == (MigrationError,)`, six errors in total, and the four direct descendants of `PersistenceError` unchanged. The separate `tests/integration/test_postgres_migration_lock_error.py` pins that only a busy lock produces `MigrationLockError`: its non-lock `MigrationError` cases must not be a `MigrationLockError`.
- **P-3 consequences, accepted by the human on 2026-10-15.** No page above the chain's valid `last-page-reached` is requested; page bodies report `pages` 9 in the other scenarios (intent AC conventions); an unjustified `first_page` and a sentinel run holding page events are malformed.
- **Validation of `last-page-reached`, required by the human on 2026-10-15.** § State machine H, step 3.
- **Confirmed definitively by the human on 2026-10-15:**
  - the new hierarchy test `tests/unit/test_persistence_error_hierarchy.py`;
  - `storage-failed` may follow a valid `last-page-reached`;
  - two `last-page-reached` events anywhere in the chain are rejected, with no precedence rule.
- **Coverage of `last-page-reached`, required by the human on 2026-10-15.** § State machine H, step 3, the coverage bullet and its consequence.

- **F-2, documentation.** The ingest execution id has 32 characters (§ Public names and signatures).

### Audit round 1 responses (2026-10-18; approved by the human on 2026-10-18)
Recorded in intent Revision 8 § Findings of the audit, round 1. No acceptance criterion is weakened; no earlier decision is reopened. Each is the most conservative rule consistent with the existing decisions.

- **F-03 (HIGH).** The source is built at invocation step 2, before the migrations; any `Exception` other than `MemoryError` from `make_source` is exit 2 with a fixed message naming the six proxy and certificate variables, never a value; nothing is written (§ Order of an invocation, § Exit codes, § Output). Rejected: checking after the migrations (writes to the database before a configuration error, against AC14); importing `httpx` in `interfaces` to catch `InvalidURL` (breaks § Layer placement); a new infrastructure factory that translates the errors (a new module for one `try`, and F2 modules must stay unchanged, AC20).
- **F-05 (MEDIUM, spec gap).** § State machine K step 1: `read_run` first; a finished run gets nothing more and keeps its status; when `read_run` fails, nothing is written. Rejected: appending first and then checking (the append would already break AC11).
- **F-10 (LOW, spec gap).** § State machine R: `OSError` and a vanished file mapped to `local-listing-failed`, `local-observation-unreadable` or `object-integrity`; the closed list is unchanged.
- **F-11 (LOW, spec gap).** § State machine P "Refused writes" and § State machine R: a database refusal is answered with at most one `storage-failed` (`postgresql`), never `unexpected-error`; a refused `finish_run(FAILED)` leaves the run `running` for AC10.
- **F-04 (MEDIUM).** Invocation step 6 and § Output: `<id> running` for every exception that escapes after `run_started`; exit 1, or 130 for Ctrl+C.
- **F-01, F-02 (CRITICAL), F-06, F-07 (MEDIUM): implementation defects against the existing text.** Clarified without a new rule: the pending local observation (P step 3), what the cleanup tolerates (K), the H message, and the required progress lines (§ Public names and signatures).
- **F-08, F-09 (LOW).** Code only (dead `_finish`; the "33 characters" docstring). No spec change.
- **F-12 (LOW).** `tasks.md` § Status, § Working state and § Checkpoint updated.

### Audit round 2 responses (2026-10-18; approved by the human on 2026-10-18)
Recorded in intent Revision 9 § Findings of the audit, round 2. No acceptance criterion is weakened; no earlier decision is reopened. Each is the most conservative rule consistent with the existing decisions.

- **F-14 (MEDIUM, spec conflict).** Intent AC15 is kept and the plan follows it: required progress 2 and 3 now cover every database operation refused or lost, read or write, in every phase of `run_ingest`, with exactly one line per failed call (§ Public names, required progress; § State machine C, T, R, H, I). Rejected: narrowing AC15 to "from `start_run` on" (weakens a criterion; the operator would not learn which operation failed, or on which run, during reconciliation).
- **F-13 (MEDIUM): implementation defect against AC15.** Clarified without a new rule: § State machine K names its four database operations and requires the line before it stops; § State machine C step 5 names the `read_run` after a refused P-2 repeat.
- **F-15 (LOW, spec gap).** A refused or lost read before the run exists ends `NOT_STARTED` (exit 1) with a fixed message naming the identity: T (the target; never `INVALID_TARGET`, because the target's existence is unknown), R (`X`, as R's refusals already did), H (the target; the message does not call the chain invalid, because no verdict was reached), I (the drawn id). Rejected: exit 2 for an unreadable target (would call an operational failure an input error); reusing `invalid resumption chain <id>` for a refused read (would report an integrity verdict that was never reached).
- **F-16 (LOW).** (a) `run_ingest` handles every exception of the first `open_repository()` (Ctrl+C -> `INTERRUPTED` with no run; anything else -> `NOT_STARTED`), so the pinned "returns an outcome" sentence holds; nothing is written; every repository it obtained is closed. (b) The result line is written at most once per invocation; a Ctrl+C while the source is closed exits 130 and adds no line (§ Order of an invocation step 7, § Output, § Exit codes). Rejected for (b): exiting with the outcome's code and ignoring the Ctrl+C (an interrupt is never swallowed elsewhere; after F-05, a Ctrl+C that lands after the run was finished already exits 130 with the stored status).
- **F-17 (LOW, documentation).** The README content is listed in § Consumers and migration "Docs"; the documentation step writes it. No AC change.
- **F-12 (LOW, carried).** `tasks.md` § Status, § Working state, § Checkpoint and the O10 rework order updated in its Revision 4.

### Audit round 3 responses (2026-10-18; approved on 2026-10-18)
Recorded in intent Revision 10 § Findings of the audit, round 3. No acceptance criterion is weakened; no earlier decision is reopened. Each is the most conservative rule consistent with the existing decisions.

- **F-18 (HIGH, spec collision).** § State machine C, "The current repository", and the first bullet of § State machine K: a repository is current only while it holds the ingest exclusion; after a loss, none is current until the second repository's `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion()` returned `True`; any exception in that window ends C with nothing read or written, the second repository closed, one line naming the operation that raised, and `running` (`INTERRUPTED` for Ctrl+C, `RUN_FAILED` otherwise). C step 3 wins over K's "the same steps 1 to 4". Rejected: letting K write on the second repository after checking `read_run` (a read proves nothing about the exclusion; another `ingest` may be reconciling the run, AC11, Q4); letting K take the exclusion itself (K opens no connection and takes no lock, Q11).
- **F-19 (LOW).** The same rule: an exception from the second `open_repository()` ends C without K, so no operation is attempted on the closed first repository, and the one line names `open_repository`, named alone (the shared rule of required progress, which the two existing C line tests pin with `assert_names_no_run`). Rejected: confirming the current `read_run failed ... (RuntimeError)` line (it names an operation that never reached the database, against AC15).
- **F-20 (LOW, recommendation).** Required now: tests for a lost `start_run` whose recovery fails (`<id> running` without `run started`, documented in the README) and for the AC6 (b) read-back whose `record_observation` is refused or lost; the non-database exception before the run exists is pinned by the `start_run` cases of the F-18 and F-19 rows. Not required: the normal-path append whose recovery fails, and the append on the new connection lost after the re-check, recorded in `tasks.md` § Carried reservations as accepted limitations (the auditor's round 3 experiments showed the planned behavior for both; X9 is the append after the re-check).
- **F-12 (LOW, carried).** Code-free: `tasks.md` § Status, § Working state, § Checkpoint and the O10 rework order updated in its Revision 5.

### Audit round 4 responses (2026-10-19; approved by the human on 2026-10-20)
Recorded in intent Revision 11 § Findings of the audit, round 4. No acceptance criterion is weakened; no earlier decision is reopened. Each is the most conservative rule consistent with the existing decisions.

- **F-21 (MEDIUM, blocking; implementation defect against the existing text, made structural).** § State machine C "The explicit record": the run session records the current repository, *none* from the first action of the handler of a `PersistenceConnectionError` until the second exclusion returned `True`; § State machine K and every read or write of the run take the repository from that record, so with none K is never entered, whatever the exception and wherever it lands; the lines (one only for a database operation that raised); C steps 1 and 3 (a `close()` that raises an `Exception` other than `MemoryError` is suppressed and the recovery goes on; a `KeyboardInterrupt` or a `MemoryError` there ends C); C step 6 (the record is *none* after a further loss); the final close attempts once more every repository whose close was interrupted. The C step 1 question of the auditor is settled for the conservative reading: an ordinary `Exception` from the close is suppressed quietly (the step's own word), and a `MemoryError` ends C, as the window bullet already said; experiment E13's behavior (suppress a `MemoryError` and continue) changes accordingly. Rejected: wrapping more calls one by one in `_recovery_step` (the defect class stays open for the next unwrapped point, as mutant R1 showed); keeping `MemoryError` at step 1 suppressed (it would keep the collision with the window bullet).
- **F-22 (LOW).** § Public names, "The final close": an `Exception` from the final close is suppressed; a `KeyboardInterrupt` there returns the determined outcome with `end=INTERRUPTED`, the same run and the same confirmed status (exit 130). Rejected: letting the interrupt escape (the command would print `<id> running` for a run whose closing was confirmed, against AC15); swallowing the interrupt with the run's own exit code (an interrupt is never swallowed elsewhere, F-16 b).
- **F-20 (LOW).** The generic `NOT_STARTED` fallback for a non-database exception before the run exists is not an accepted limitation: the AC15 (F-20, the generic fallback) row requires the `generic_fallback` test (experiment E6 shows the planned behavior, and mutant R11 survives today). The accepted limitations stay the two of Revision 8 (`tasks.md` § Carried reservations).
- **F-12 (LOW, carried).** Code-free: `tasks.md` § Status, § Working state, § Checkpoint, § Finding responses and O10 updated in its Revision 6, including the human's chat instructions of rounds 1 to 3 on who corrects `run_ingest`.

### Sweep A2 responses (2026-10-20; approved by the human on 2026-10-20)
Recorded in intent Revision 12 § Findings of the architect's sweep A2. No acceptance criterion is weakened and no earlier decision is reopened (F-01 to F-22, the accepted inconsistency of the `start_run` window, the two accepted limitations of F-20, Q1 to Q15). Each rule is the one that matches the approved behavior, and each of N-1 to N-4 and N-7 has a § Validation row.

- **N-1.** § State machine C step 6: the `start_run` window's outcomes hold when the repeat of `start_run` is lost too. The code behaves this way at `beadcf8` (lines 266 and 1296-1302, then 1528-1530). The row protects the round 5 restructuring.
- **N-2.** § Public names (`run_ingest`) and § State machine K: any `KeyboardInterrupt` during K propagates, whatever K was entered for (exit 130, `<id> running`). Chosen against "return `INTERRUPTED` with `running`" because it matches AC7's "the cleanup cannot finish", P-5, K's existing "a second `KeyboardInterrupt` propagates", and the code (`_cleanup_step` and K's handler catch `Exception` only). Both choices give the same exit code and stdout. The human accepted the propagation on 2026-10-20 (the review of the draft).
- **N-3.** § State machine C, "Two or more exceptions": a `KeyboardInterrupt` among them decides `INTERRUPTED`. Otherwise the other exception decides, and it prevails over a `False` or a `PersistenceError`. This agrees with "The final close" (F-22).
- **N-4.** § State machine P step 3 and "A refusal is answered, never retried", and § State machine K step 2. The code reading at `beadcf8` is in the `refusal_not_retried` row and in `tasks.md` § Working state. It found that the code does retry a refused write in four places: lines 1433-1441, 1387-1395, the refused repeat on the second repository that reaches those handlers, and 477-484 for `finish_run(FAILED)`. This is an implementation defect for the round 5 fix, not a change of the earlier rule (F-11). The human accepted the rule on 2026-10-20, including case (iv), the refused `finish_run(FAILED)`.
- **N-7.** § Public names (`run_ingest_command`), invocation step 6, § Exit codes and § Output, "Writing the result". The confirmed status is kept, the result line is attempted at most once, PostgreSQL is not touched, a Ctrl+C there exits 130, and any other exception that prevents writing the result exits 1 (130 is kept when already determined). The exit code is the human's decision of 2026-10-20, which amends the draft's "keep the exit code already determined". A complete line is not guaranteed when the stream fails.
- **N-5, N-6** are intent-only (AC7, AC15). **N-8**: C step 5, the P-2 bullet. **N-9**: § Public names, the list of what propagates out of `run_ingest`. **N-10**: the seams table, the close fault is one-shot. **N-11**: `tasks.md` only.
- **Identified for the audit, not in scope of this correction.** After a refused failure-path append (`page-failed` or `storage-failed`), a Ctrl+C that lands in its line still makes the cleanup append `run-interrupted`. That is another write, not a retry of the refused one, so the rule "a refusal is answered, never retried" does not cover it. No rule, row or fix is part of Revision 10; the auditor of round 5 is asked to look at it.

- **F-31 (D-11 and D-12 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-27).** § Public names: "A missing stream" under "Protected writes and the last flush" (with its table), "D-5 as adjusted for a missing stream" under `interfaces.cli`, the note on `run_ingest_command`'s `stdout` and `stderr`; § Output, "Writing the result"; § Exit codes (the note under the table); § Binding constraints "Tests" (two seams, the Revision 13 tokens); § Ownership (`cli.py` and the `cli` capability); § Consumers and migration (the tests touched by Revision 13, the README sentence); § Validation (six rows, the Revision 13 additions to the process tier with its cleanup on a timeout); § Risks (one row). The code change is in `interfaces` only: `output_streams.py` (a missing stream in the protected write, the neutralizing and the last flush) and `cli.py` (the two hooks of the one local parser class); `ingest_command.py` needs no change beyond what the protected write does for it, apart from the suggested annotations. `application` is not touched.
- **D-5.** Adjusted, not reopened, the adjustments (a) to (d) accepted by the human on 2026-10-27: the class also overrides `print_usage`, its writing hook drops a missing stream instead of falling back to the other one, and its `AttributeError` suppression stays for a stream object without `write`. Option a (an `OSError` reaches `main`'s handler), the D-5 tests and `tests/unit/test_cli.py` are unchanged.
- **The mutants (g) and (n).** Left out by the human on 2026-10-27: no row of § Validation for them; they remain class B reservations pending the human's decision (F-36).

### Rollout order (for `tasks.md` to slice into outcomes)
1. **M1, the F1 listing.** Port operation and `LocalDiskRawEvidenceStore` implementation with `test_local_disk_raw_evidence_store_listing.py` (AC22, AC20). It lands first because both reconciliation and the id check need it.
2. **M2, the F3 extension.** `list_runs`, `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion` and `INGEST_EXCLUSION_KEY`, with `test_postgres_runs_and_exclusion.py` (AC23, AC20); and the new error `MigrationLockError` with its runner branch, the one added assertion in `tests/integration/test_postgres_migrations.py`, `tests/unit/test_persistence_error_hierarchy.py` and `tests/integration/test_postgres_migration_lock_error.py` (AC16, AC20, P-1).
3. **M3, the vocabulary.** `domain/ingestion_catalogue.py`, `application/ingest_ports.py`, and the types of `ingest_use_case.py` (requests, `IngestEnd`, `IngestOutcome`, the `run_ingest` stub), `infrastructure/system_clock.py`, and the doubles in `tests/unit/ingest/conftest.py`. The test-writer then writes every F4 test in red against these names (gate 2, Day 19).
4. **M4 🧑, the use case.** The human implements `run_ingest` in `application` by hand (Day 19: "Implementa tú el caso de uso en application", intent § Constraints), against the unit tests with doubles (AC1 to AC13, AC17, AC24). The executor does not write this module's body; it may review and point to failing tests.
5. **M5, the CLI.** `interfaces/ingest_command.py` and the `cli.py` subparser (AC14, AC15, AC16, AC25), unit tests first, then integration and CLI tests (AC8, AC10, AC19).
6. **M6, documentation.** AC21 and the `harny-sync` notes. The human writes `docs/learning/journal.md` in his own words: the Day 17 "Siguiente paso" correction and the Day 18 entry when the three specs close; later days as they happen.
7. **After gate 3.** The Day 20 live run (Q10) is the human's manual step, outside the suite.

- **Docs.**
  - `README.md` § Technologies and § Development commands: the `ingest` command and the rule that it is run from the repository root; `uv run --env-file .env ec-procurement-quality ingest ...`; `--resume`; the fixed `data/raw/`; the optional `POSTGRES_HOST` and `POSTGRES_PORT`; the exit codes 0, 1, 2 and 130; how to end a stale session that holds the exclusion (a `psql` query on `pg_locks` with `locktype = 'advisory'` and `pg_terminate_backend`, documented without the key's value); the sentences "nothing calls ... yet" replaced.
  - `README.md` § Ingesting, required by Revision 7 (audit finding F-17; written by the documentation step, not by the architect):
    - every case that prints `<id> running`, which means that closing the run was not confirmed (decision P-5), and that the next `ingest` reconciles and closes such a run:
      - a Ctrl+C or a `MemoryError` whose cleanup could not finish: a second Ctrl+C, the cleanup could not read the run, or one of its database operations was refused or lost;
      - a lost database connection that could not be recovered: the second connection could not be opened, the ingest exclusion was held elsewhere or could not be taken again, or the connection was lost a second time, including a `start_run` whose outcome is unknown;
      - a refused `finish_run(failed)`;
      - a refused repeat of the final `finish_run(succeeded)` after a lost connection, when the stored status could not then be read;
      - any exception that escaped after the run was announced;
      - required by Revision 8 (audit finding F-18; written by the documentation step): a Ctrl+C or an unexpected error such as `MemoryError` while a lost connection was being recovered (the second connection being opened or the ingest exclusion being taken again), after which nothing is written to the run; while a lost `start_run` is being recovered, a Ctrl+C or an unexpected error prints no result line, because no run was confirmed;
      - required by Revision 9 (audit finding F-21; written by the documentation step): that case covers the whole recovery, from the moment the connection is lost until the ingest exclusion is held on the second connection, including while the lost connection is closed and while a second connection that could not take the exclusion is closed; stderr then names at most `open_repository` or `try_acquire_ingest_exclusion`, and no line names an operation that was not attempted. (The operator-visible change is limited to rare double faults: a `MemoryError` while the lost connection is closed now leaves the run `running` instead of closing it `failed`, and the misleading `read_run failed` line disappears. F-22 needs no README change: the result line keeps the status the process confirmed, as the README already says.)
    - in the exit-code table, the exit-2 row names a malformed proxy or certificate setting of the environment (`HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`), next to the missing or invalid variables; the exit-1 row says that a resumption target the database could not read (refused, or connection lost) is exit 1, not 2;
    - stderr names every database operation that was refused or lost, with the run concerned (fixed text, at most an error's class name).
    - required by Revision 12 (audit finding F-27, amended on 2026-10-23 before approval; written by the documentation step): one brief README note that tells the normal behavior of a closed or failing output stream (exit 1, or 130, never Python's own exit 120 or its `Exception ignored` report, and the stored run keeps its status) from the residual case of decision D-10 (if the neutralizing of the failed stream itself fails, Python's finalization may still print its report and replace the agreed code by 120, with no effect on PostgreSQL and no second attempt of the output).
    - required by Revision 13 (audit finding F-31; written by the documentation step): the one short sentence of § Consumers and migration, "F4 tests touched by Revision 13", on a stream that is missing at start-up.
  - `AGENTS.md` § Current commands: the ingest command.
  - `docs/architecture.md` § Open questions: the minimum audit events and the scope answered for ingestion; § Infrastructure boundary: the ingestion now uses the database.
  - `CHANGELOG.md` § Unreleased: the `ingest` command (Added); the two port extensions and `MigrationLockError` (Changed; the runner still raises a `MigrationError`, now the subclass for a busy lock). Known limitations:
    - the best-effort cleanup;
    - a stale session can hold the exclusion;
    - a page whose `page-completed` event was lost is requested again;
    - the command is supported only from the repository root;
    - the commit-in-flight case is still not tested against PostgreSQL.
  - `docs/learning/journal.md`: 🧑.
- **Consumers (F5 and later).** F4 leaves:
  - every response as an observation in both stores;
  - the `page-completed` events, which name the sequence and content hash of each complete page, the only F4 statement that a `200` body passed F2's checks;
  - the `run-started.resumes` chain;
  - the scope.

  F5 should take its inputs from `page-completed` and not from every `200` observation (F2 consumer rule), and must keep normalized records free of duplicates when a scope is repeated (intent § Constraints, the human's rule). `api/record` is not fetched by F4 (Q15). Nothing here designs F5.

## Risks
| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Carried, persistence U1: a lost commit leaves the outcome unknown, and the real commit-in-flight case cannot be made deterministic against PostgreSQL | Low | Med | § State machine C repeats only safe operations and checks before re-appending an event; unit tests simulate commit-then-raise with `FlakyRepository`; integration covers the deterministic loss cases |
| Carried, raw-evidence N2: `OSError` after publication | Low | Med | P step 3 asks the port and records a published observation (AC6 b) |
| Carried, source-access R-6 and raw-evidence N3: an exception chain holds protocol text or a record | Low | Med | Only `str()` of project errors and class names of other exceptions are printed; AC15 markers |
| Carried, R-7: `httpcore` at `DEBUG` writes header values | Low | Med | Invocation step 2; AC15 test lowers the root logger to `DEBUG` first |
| Carried, R-11: one source shared or re-entered | Low | High | One source per invocation, one thread; the AC25 chdir handler does not call the source |
| Carried, source-access U1: the real portal answers differently | Med | Low | Day 20 live run by the human; every response kept and typed |
| Carried, persistence N5: reconciliation reads run in separate snapshots | Low | Low | Safe because the exclusion keeps every other ingest out |
| New: the integration guard's allowance of `getaddrinfo` for the database host depends on `psycopg` internals (§ Guidance consulted) | Low | Low | `uv.lock` pins `psycopg` 3.3.6; the guard proof test fails loudly if `httpx` gets through or PostgreSQL is blocked |
| New: `os.chdir` in the AC25 handler is process-wide and could leak into other tests | Med | Low | `monkeypatch.chdir` restores it; that test does not run in parallel (the suite is serial) |
| New: after a second Ctrl+C, stdout `<id> running` may be wrong if `finish_run` had committed just before | Low | Low | Documented as "last status confirmed by the process; closing not confirmed" (decision P-5, § Output); AC10 fixes the state on the next run |
| New (P-2): exit 0 after a database failure could hide an unconfirmed finish | Low | Med | Exit 0 only when the exact repeat of the final `finish_run` returns the stored `succeeded` run with the same `finished_at`; every other loss is exit 1 (§ State machine C); stderr still reports the lost connection |
| New (P-1): a future runner change raises `MigrationLockError` for a case that is not the busy lock, or the reverse | Low | Med | `test_postgres_migration_lock_error.py` pins the only lock case and the non-lock cases by exact type |
| New: a stale backend keeps the exclusion after a host crash | Low | Med | README procedure; the refusal writes nothing |
| New (Revision 6, F-03): the broad catch around `make_source` reports a defect that is not an environment setting as configuration (exit 2) | Low | Low | With `default_wiring` every constructor argument is a code constant pinned by tests, so only the environment can make construction fail; the message carries the class name; nothing is written either way |
| New: the in-memory doubles drift from the real adapters' rules | Med | Med | Doubles implement PS-7 to PS-9 and F1's validation; the integration end-to-end rows re-run the main paths on the real adapters |
| New (Revision 13, F-31): the parser class now overrides two private-contract methods of `argparse` (`_print_message`, `print_usage`), whose callers differ between Python versions (3.14.9 read locally; 3.13 `unavailable` locally) | Low | Low | Both overrides keep `argparse`'s default meaning of a call without a file; the `absent_*` and `process_no_*` tests run on 3.14 locally and on 3.13 in CI, and the check that `argparse` itself is not patched covers both methods |

## Validation
Cwd: the repository root for every row. Setup for every row: `uv sync --locked`. Integration rows also need the Compose database (`docker compose up -d --wait`) and run with `--env-file .env`; each test runs in its own throwaway database (PT-13). Unit rows run under the `tests/unit/ingest/conftest.py` guard and integration rows under the `tests/integration/ingest/conftest.py` guard. No row touches the real `data/raw/` or the database named by `POSTGRES_DB`. Broader commands:
- unit rows: `uv run pytest --cov=ec_procurement_quality --cov-report=term-missing`;
- integration rows: `uv run --env-file .env pytest --cov=ec_procurement_quality --cov-report=term-missing`.

| AC | Demonstrated by | Tests to write | Tier | Framework | Setup | Focused command | Broader command | Cwd |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AC1 | a two-page scope ends `succeeded` with both stores and events in order | `success` (unit, doubles): requests 1 then 2, sequences 1 and 2, events `run-started`, `page-completed` x2, `finish_run(SUCCEEDED)`, outcome `SUCCEEDED`; `e2e_success` (integration): through `run_ingest_command` with the simulated source, the real store on `tmp_path` and the migrated database; files `observations/<A>/1.json`, `2.json`; rows by direct SQL; exit 0; stdout `<A> succeeded` | unit, integration | pytest, httpx `MockTransport`, psycopg | doubles; migrated database | `uv run pytest tests/unit/ingest -q -k success`; `uv run --env-file .env pytest tests/integration/ingest -q -k e2e_success` | both broader commands | repo root |

| AC24 (`last-page-reached`) | no stored `last-page-reached` limits a resumption unless it is valid; an invalid one makes the chain malformed and never makes a page be skipped | `chain_last_page` (unit, doubles; each case built by appending events through the in-memory repository): run A, scope 1 to 4, `failed`, with `page-completed` for pages 1 and 2, then one of: detail `{"page": 2}` (missing key), `{"page": 2, "pages": 2, "x": 1}` (extra key), `{"page": True, "pages": 1}`, `{"page": 2, "pages": False}`, `{"page": 5, "pages": 2}` (above `last_page`), `{"page": 2, "pages": 3}` (`page < pages`), `{"page": 2, "pages": -1}`; two events in A; one valid event in A and another in a later run B of the chain; an event for page 3 with no `page-completed` for page 3; an event for page 2 whose `page-completed` for page 2 comes only after it; a valid event followed in A by a `page-completed` or by a `page-failed`; coverage: run A, scope 1 to 4, with valid `page-completed` for pages 1 and 4 only, then `last-page-reached` (page 4, `pages` 4) and no `page-completed` for page 2 or 3 anywhere in the chain.
