| AC14, AC15, AC19 (F-31, real process; D-11 and D-12 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-27) | a child that really starts without a stderr, a stdout or both (CPython sets `None`), and the bootstrap's emulation, give the codes and the stdout of the unit rows: no stderr text on stdout, no `AttributeError`, no substitute stream | The process tier with its Revision 13 additions (the section below this table), `tests/process/test_cli_process_streams.py`; every case runs in two modes, `closed` (the launcher closes the descriptor before the bootstrap starts) and `emulated` (the bootstrap sets the stream to `None` itself), and the two must agree. `process_no_stderr`: the stub returning `SUCCEEDED` with `succeeded`, `RUN_FAILED` with `failed`, `INTERRUPTED` with `failed`, and raising `KeyboardInterrupt` after `run_started` -> 0, 1, 130 and 130, stdout (read by the parent) exactly `<A> succeeded`, `<A> failed`, `<A> failed` and `<A> running`, once; `ingest --last-page 2` and the bare command -> 2, stdout empty; `POSTGRES_DB` absent from the child's environment -> 2, stdout empty; `--version` -> 0, stdout exactly the version text; `--help` -> 0, stdout starting with `usage:`; the parent's stderr empty in every case. `process_no_stdout`: the stub `SUCCEEDED` -> 0 and `INTERRUPTED` -> 130, stderr holding `run <A> started` and `synthetic outcome message` and no `<A> ` result line; `--version` and `--help` -> 0, stderr empty; `ingest --last-page 2` -> 2 with `usage:` and `error:` on stderr; `POSTGRES_DB` absent -> 2 with `error: POSTGRES_DB is not set`; the parent's stdout empty and no record file. `process_no_streams`: the stub `SUCCEEDED` -> 0, `INTERRUPTED` -> 130, `ingest --last-page 2` and `POSTGRES_DB` absent -> 2, `--version` -> 0; both parent pipes empty. In every case: the start-state record shows the case's missing streams as `None`, no exception other than `SystemExit` left `cli.main` (no `escaped` record), the exact code is never 96, 97, 98 or 120, and no stream the parent reads holds `Exception ignored`, `flushing sys.`, `Traceback` or `AttributeError`. Expected to pass at `871f440`, recorded as passing: `--version` and `--help` with a missing stderr; `INTERRUPTED`, the parse error and `POSTGRES_DB` absent with a missing stdout; the parse error and `--version` with both missing. | process | pytest, `subprocess` | the bootstrap and the launcher of the process tier; `tmp_path` as the child's current directory | `uv run pytest tests/process -q -k process_no_`; `uv run pytest tests/process -q` | unit broader (it includes `tests/process/`) | repo root |
| AC19 (F-31, the cleanup of both processes on a timeout; the requirement the human added on 2026-10-27 when accepting the launcher) | a child that never ends leaves no live or unreaped process: the launcher's shorter timeout kills and reaps it, and the parent's timeout kills both by handle and pid, reaps the launcher and checks that neither is alive before it fails | `process_hung_cleanup` (process tier, `tests/process/test_cli_process_streams.py`; two cases, each in the `closed` mode with descriptor 2 closed; the timeouts shortened through `monkeypatch` of the module-level constants of the cleanup rule; the bootstrap's `hang` case reads the go byte and then blocks forever without calling `cli.main`). (1) *The launcher's timeout*: the launcher's 2 s, the parent's 10 s -> the parent's `communicate` returns with exit code 96 (the test asserts 96 itself, not through `assert_ended`), no timeout of the parent; the pid in the pid file is not alive within the liveness deadline. (2) *The parent's timeout*: the launcher started with the test-only stall switch (it writes the pid file and then blocks instead of waiting), the launcher's 2 s, the parent's 4 s -> the harness's timeout path runs, and the test catches its failure (`pytest.raises(pytest.fail.Exception)`, the message naming the case and saying both processes were cleaned up); afterwards the launcher's `returncode` is set, and the launcher and the pid of the pid file are not alive (checked again by the test with the same liveness helper). Cost: about 7 s for both (experiment: 2.1 s and 4.0 s plus start-up). Not flaky by construction: no sleep synchronizes anything; the pid file is written atomically before the launcher waits and is read only after a timeout; the timeouts are far above Python's start-up time; liveness is polled until a deadline (5 s), never checked once; a Linux zombie waiting for a reaper counts as gone; the reaping `communicate` has its own bound, so a leak fails the test instead of hanging the suite, and the harness kills the leaked pid before it fails, so even a failing run leaves no process. Expected at `871f440`: new, and green as soon as it is written, because it exercises the harness and the bootstrap's `hang` case, not the product (before the harness exists there is nothing to collect, which is not a valid red reason). Its value is shown on scratch mutants of the harness, never in the repository, recorded by the test-writer: without the parent's kill of the child pid the test must fail (experiment, Windows: the child stayed alive and the reaping `communicate` timed out after 10 s; the leaked process was then killed by hand), and with the launcher's timeout not shorter than the parent's, or without the launcher's own `kill()` and `wait()`, case (1) must fail | process | pytest, `subprocess`, `os`, `signal`, `ctypes` (Windows liveness only; standard library) | the launcher, the bootstrap's `hang` case and the cleanup rule of the process tier; `tmp_path` | `uv run pytest tests/process -q -k process_hung_cleanup` | unit broader (it includes `tests/process/`) | repo root |

The executor's surviving mutants (g) and (n) have no row: the human left their tests out on 2026-10-27, and they remain class B reservations pending the human's decision (F-36).

**Process tier** (Revision 12, audit finding F-27; new, authorized by the human on 2026-10-22; D-8 and D-9, option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-22). Tests in `tests/process/test_cli_process_streams.py`, run by the default `uv run pytest` and by CI's `Tests` job (the `testpaths` cover `tests/`). No new marker and no `pyproject.toml` change (AC18, AC20); no `conftest.py`. Each test:
- writes a bootstrap script into `tmp_path` and starts `[sys.executable, <bootstrap>, <case>, *argv]` with `subprocess.Popen`, `stdin`, `stdout` and `stderr` all `subprocess.PIPE`, `cwd=tmp_path`, no shell and no `creationflags`. The environment is the parent's with `PYTHONUNBUFFERED` removed (so stdout is block-buffered, as in a real run), `POSTGRES_HOST=db.invalid` and synthetic `POSTGRES_DB`, `POSTGRES_USER` and `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`. The project is importable because `uv sync` installs it into the `.venv` whose interpreter runs the tests;
- synchronizes without sleeping, and collects the child with `communicate` only (the exact pattern, required):
  1. The child's first action is to block reading one byte from its stdin (the go byte); it writes nothing before it.
  2. Right after `Popen`, for each pipe the case breaks, the parent closes its own read end and sets the matching attribute to `None` (`proc.stdout.close(); proc.stdout = None`, the same for `proc.stderr`), so that `communicate` neither reads nor touches that end. The pipe is therefore closed before the child can write.
  3. The parent then makes one call, `out, err = proc.communicate(input=b"g", timeout=60)`: it sends the go byte, closes stdin, reads the pipes it kept to their end and waits for the child. No other read, `wait()` or `poll()` comes before it, and none is a blocking read of its own. A broken pipe yields `None` for that stream.
  4. On `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` the parent calls `proc.kill()` and then `proc.communicate()` to collect the child and its pipes, and only then fails the test, so no hung or unreaped child is left.

  The pattern was exercised on this Windows machine on 2026-10-22 with a scratch child in the session scratchpad, never in the repository (label `experiment`, Windows, Python 3.13.9): no broken pipe (exit 0, both texts read), stdout closed, stderr closed, and both closed, three runs each, gave no error or hang in the parent. With plain `print`s the child ended with 120 in every closed case, with `Exception ignored on flushing sys.stdout: OSError: [Errno 22] Invalid argument` for a closed stdout and silently for a closed stderr. A child that writes and flushes in one attempt and redirects the failed descriptor to `os.devnull` ended with 1. A child that never ends was killed after the timeout and collected by the second `communicate`. Linux is expected to behave the same (`BrokenPipeError` instead of `OSError`); it is checked only by CI;
- asserts the exact exit code, which is never 120 and never 97 or 98 (below), and that no stream the parent reads contains `Exception ignored`, `flushing sys.` or `Traceback`. These cases are the normal case, where the null device can be opened and copied onto the descriptor; the process tier does not break the neutralizing, whose failure is the D-10 unit row and the residual limitation of § Public names.

The bootstrap (written by the test; it never touches the real source, database or `data/raw/`):
- installs a socket guard: `socket.socket.connect`, `connect_ex`, `socket.create_connection` and `socket.getaddrinfo` end the child at once with `os._exit(97)`;
- replaces `sys.stdout` with a recording text stream over the same descriptor 1: an `io.TextIOWrapper` subclass over `io.BufferedWriter(io.FileIO(1, "w", closefd=False))`, with the original encoding and errors, `line_buffering=False` and `write_through=False`, as Python builds it for a pipe. It appends every text it is handed to `stdout-record.txt` in `tmp_path` (opened, appended and closed for each text) and then passes it on, so the parent can check the single attempt and the status of a line that the closed pipe never delivered;
- for an ingest case, replaces `ingest_command.run_ingest` with a stub of the pinned signature and `cli.default_wiring` with a stub wiring:
  - the stub calls `reporter.run_started("ingest-20261014t150000z-0a1b2c3d")` unless the case says not to, then returns the case's `IngestOutcome` (message `synthetic outcome message`) or raises the case's exception;
  - in the wiring, `make_source` returns a dummy source whose `close` raises only when the case says so, `migrate` returns `()`, `make_store` returns a dummy that is never written, and `make_repository` ends the child with `os._exit(98)` if it is ever called;
- then calls `cli.main(argv)` and lets the process end through Python's normal exit, so the exit-time flush is exercised.

Platforms: the same tests run on Windows and Linux. A pipe whose reader has gone makes the write or flush raise `OSError` (`EINVAL`) on Windows and `BrokenPipeError` (`EPIPE`) on Linux; the child's interpreter ignores `SIGPIPE` from start-up, so it is not killed by the signal (`Popen`'s `restore_signals` does not change that). The only platform-dependent assertion is the class name in `unexpected error: <cls>` (`OSError` or `BrokenPipeError`). Evidence: the tests are executed locally on Windows (label `rerun`, Windows) and on Linux only by CI's `Tests` run on the head of PR #26 (label `reused`, CI on Linux); no record claims a local Linux run, and each record names its platform.

**Process tier, Revision 13: missing streams** (audit finding F-31; D-11 and D-12 option A, chosen by the human on 2026-10-27; the exceptions below accepted by the human on 2026-10-27 with the cleanup requirement; required once the revision is approved). The same file, the same harness pattern and the same rules, with these additions:
- *The bootstrap* gains five things, each inert when both streams are present and no switch or case of its own is set, so the 26 existing tests are unaffected:
  1. right after the go byte, an emulation switch (an environment variable set by the parent for the `emulated` mode; its name is free and synthetic) makes the bootstrap set `sys.stdout`, `sys.stderr` or both to `None` itself, before it imports the project;
  2. it then writes the start state, whether `sys.stdout` and `sys.stderr` are `None`, to a record file in `tmp_path` (for example `stream-state.txt`), and the parent asserts it equals the case's missing streams, so a platform where CPython does not set `None` makes the test fail instead of passing vacuously;
  3. it replaces `sys.stdout` with the recording stream only when `sys.stdout` is not `None` (today it reads `sys.stdout.encoding`, which fails for `None`);
  4. it calls `cli.main(argv)` inside a `try` that lets `SystemExit` through untouched and, for any other exception, writes its class name to a record file (for example `escaped.txt`) before raising it again, because with a missing stderr a traceback would be invisible; the parent asserts that the file does not exist. The process still ends through Python's normal exit.
  5. a `hang` case, used only by `process_hung_cleanup`: right after the go byte the bootstrap blocks forever, without importing the project or calling `cli.main`.
- *The launcher* (the `closed` mode; written by the test into `tmp_path`, like the bootstrap). The parent starts `[sys.executable, <launcher>, <pid file>, <launcher timeout>, <descriptors>, sys.executable, <bootstrap>, <case>, *argv]` with exactly the harness pattern above (three pipes, `cwd=tmp_path`, the environment, no shell, no `creationflags`, the go byte, one `communicate(input=b"g", timeout=PARENT_TIMEOUT)`). The launcher writes nothing on any stream. It closes the named descriptors (`os.close(1)`, `os.close(2)` or both), starts the bootstrap with `subprocess.Popen([...])`, with no shell, no `creationflags` and no redirection, so the bootstrap inherits the parent's stdin and every descriptor still open and CPython starts it with the closed ones missing; it then writes the bootstrap's pid to the pid file in `tmp_path` (atomically: a temporary name, then `os.replace`) **before** it waits; then `wait(timeout=<launcher timeout>)`. On its own `TimeoutExpired` it calls the child's `kill()` and then `wait()` (the child is killed and reaped) and ends with `os._exit(96)`; otherwise it ends with `os._exit` of the child's code. A test-only stall switch (an argument, used only by `process_hung_cleanup`) makes it block after writing the pid file instead of waiting. Verified on this machine (experiment, Windows, Python 3.14.9, scratch, three runs each): closing 2, 1 or both gives the bootstrap `sys.stderr`, `sys.stdout` or both `None`; the go byte arrives; the parent reads empty bytes from a closed descriptor. Linux is `unavailable` locally: there the closed descriptor is inherited as closed (a pipe that `subprocess` opens inside the launcher is non-inheritable), which only CI shows; the start-state record catches a platform that differs.
- *The cleanup on a timeout* (required; the requirement the human added on 2026-10-27 when accepting the launcher). Module-level constants of the test file hold the bounds, so that `process_hung_cleanup` can shorten them through `monkeypatch`: `PARENT_TIMEOUT` (60, the existing bound of the one `communicate`), `LAUNCHER_TIMEOUT` (50), `REAP_TIMEOUT` (10) and `GONE_DEADLINE` (5); names free, the relation required: the launcher's timeout is strictly shorter than the parent's (the harness asserts `LAUNCHER_TIMEOUT < PARENT_TIMEOUT` before it starts a launcher), so in the normal hung case the launcher kills and reaps its child first and the parent sees 96. On the parent's own `TimeoutExpired` (the launcher itself does not end), in this order, then and only then the test fails:
  1. read the pid file; when it is absent (the launcher never got that far), there is no child pid and only the launcher is cleaned up;
  2. kill the child by that pid first, while the launcher is still alive and has not reaped it, so that the pid cannot have been reused: `os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)` on Linux and `os.kill(pid, signal.SIGTERM)` on Windows (`getattr(signal, "SIGKILL", signal.SIGTERM)`; on Windows `os.kill` with any signal other than the two console events ends the process with `TerminateProcess`). A pid that is already gone is not an error: the `OSError` that `os.kill` raises for it (`ProcessLookupError` on Linux; `OSError` on Windows, experiment) is ignored;
  3. kill the launcher with the `Popen` object's `kill()`;
  4. reap the launcher and drain its pipes with `communicate(timeout=REAP_TIMEOUT)`; if that times out too (a process still holds a pipe), kill the child pid again, call the launcher's `wait(timeout=REAP_TIMEOUT)`, and record the leak;
  5. check that neither process is still alive, polling until `GONE_DEADLINE` because termination and reaping are asynchronous: the launcher's `returncode` is set and its pid is not alive, and the child's pid is not alive. *Alive* means: on Windows, `OpenProcess` through `ctypes` (standard library; `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION`) succeeds and `GetExitCodeProcess` reports `STILL_ACTIVE` (259), an open that fails meaning gone (never `os.kill(pid, 0)` on Windows, which would end the process); on Linux, `os.kill(pid, 0)` does not raise `ProcessLookupError` and `/proc/<pid>/stat` does not show state `Z` (a zombie waiting for a reaper counts as gone). A process still alive at the deadline is killed once more by pid, and the failure names it;
  6. `pytest.fail` with the case and the cleanup result. No other wait or sleep is added to the harness.

  Verified on this machine (experiment, Windows, Python 3.14.9, scratch, three runs each): a child that never ends behind a launcher with a 2 s timeout and a parent with 10 s -> exit 96 after 2.1 s, the child gone; a stalled launcher with a parent timeout of 4 s -> the parent's path killed the child by pid and the launcher, reaped the launcher after 4.0 s, and neither was alive; a second `os.kill` of the gone pid raised `OSError`, which is ignored. Without step 2 (a scratch mutant) the child stayed alive and the reaping `communicate` itself timed out after 10 s, because the child still held the stdout pipe; the leaked process was killed by hand and no process was left on the machine. Linux is `unavailable` locally (CI).
- *Exceptions to the harness pattern, accepted by the human on 2026-10-27 with the cleanup requirement above* (no new tier, marker, `conftest.py`, dependency or `pyproject.toml` change): (1) a `closed` case runs two processes, bounded by the launcher's shorter timeout and, if the launcher itself does not end, by the cleanup on the parent's timeout; (2) the parent keeps all three pipes: no read end is closed by the parent (step 2 of the pattern is not used), and the pipe of a descriptor the launcher closed reads as empty, which the test asserts as "nothing written in place of the missing stream"; (3) 96 joins 97 and 98 as codes that must never be the result (except in `process_hung_cleanup`, case (1), which asserts it).
- *Platforms.* No assertion of these cases depends on the platform: a missing stream raises nothing, so no class name appears. The codes and texts must match the in-process rows (`absent_*`).
